**Arms** (all but `exact` are the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and follow-up 4's `all` faults with the false rate set as named): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget` and `long+fix+filter` (no faults); the forgetting map at **0.001, 0.003, 0.01 and 0.03** (`all@0.001+forget`, `all@0.003+forget`, **`all@0.01+forget` (designated)**, `all@0.03+forget`); and the filtered-fix sticky map (K = 0.2) at **0.0001, 0.0003 and 0.001** (`all@0.0001+filter`, `all@0.0003+filter`, `all@0.001+filter`). Ten arms, 1,800 flights. The sticky filtered arm is flown only up to 0.001: it is expected to be long dead above it, and the rates below are where its cliff is.

**One study, one new set, nothing else changed.** Everything else is as in follow-ups 3 and 4. **The set is HELD11 = seeds 1101 to 1160 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE. The faults come from follow-up 4's stream (seeded by the scenario and the arm), so the fix errors are unchanged.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated arm, the forgetting map at **0.01, ten times follow-up 4's rate**, reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind**. **SUPPORTED** if it does in all three kinds (the map has a tenfold margin), **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if in at least two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless a kind is named; each with its reason).**
* **F1** the forgetting map at 0.001 holds the bar in every kind (a replication of follow-up 4 on fresh seeds).
* **F2** and at 0.003: `all@0.003+forget` holds the bar in every kind. (Five phantom cells a ping cover about a tenth of the sector for the one ping they last, and the plan is rebuilt each ping from a map that has already forgotten the last ones.)
* **F3** and it gives out by 0.03: `all@0.03+forget` misses the bar in at least one kind. (Fifty phantoms a ping, each blocking a disc 36 m across, cover most of the sector's area; the plan changes completely at every ping.)
* **F4** the cost is monotone: from 0.001 to 0.003 to 0.01 to 0.03 the forgetting map's pooled count never rises by more than 3 flights from one rate to the next.
* **F5** a filtered fix does not rescue a sticky map from false returns: `all@0.001+filter` reaches at least 60 fewer than `all@0.001+forget`. (A filter smooths the position fix; it does nothing to a phantom cell that never clears.)
* **F6** at a rate low enough the sticky map survives: `all@0.0001+filter` reaches at least 80% of what `exact` reaches over the three kinds together. (About 24 phantoms over a whole flight.)
* **F7** and the cliff is steep: `all@0.0003+filter` reaches at least 15 fewer than `all@0.0001+filter`.
* **F8** at the shared rate 0.001 the forgetting map beats the filtered sticky one in EACH kind (`all@0.001+forget` reaches more than `all@0.001+filter` in hard-islands, in hard-gaps and in hard-current).

**What it would not say.** That 0.01 or 0.03 is the real sonar's rate or that false returns are white (follow-up 5 asks what persistence does; real ones also cluster, on a thermocline or a school of fish, in space). The other three faults are at follow-up 4's single size and the rate is swept alone: no interaction with the miss rate or the heading error is separated (the faults are all on together at every rate). The filtered fix is the only other cure flown; a map that forgets only after k consecutive free readings (hysteresis), a gate on the number of cells that change at a ping, or a median of the last few pings are untried. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 6 (2026-11-02; DEV flown once after the module and the study command and both gates were sabotaged, BEFORE HELD11 was flown; seeds 1 to 10 of each kind, 30 scenarios, 300 flights; DEV is not a result and nothing registered above was changed by it)

**What the build found, said before the set is read.** (1) The arms are follow-up 4's `all` preset with only its false rate changed, flown under `long+fix+forget` or `long+fix+filter` with the 200 m sonar; `all@0.001+forget` is follow-up 4's `all+forget` in every field of its flights but the name (the gate flies both and compares them), which is the determinism check that nothing else moved: its DEV flights are follow-up 4's (27 of 30). (2) The faults draw from the same stream at every rate (the seed is the scenario's and the sensed arm's), so a higher rate is the same uniforms against a bigger threshold: the arms are coupled and a difference between them is the rate, not luck of the draw (the draws that follow a different map are not the same, so the coupling is partial). (3) Nothing else in the module changed; the older goldens pass unchanged. The module's 40 new mutants are all red by name (one was malformed, rewritten and re-run), the study command's 81 are 76 red and 5 caught by not finishing; both in the gates' headers.

**The DEV flights** (reached of 30, three kinds together): `exact` 29, `long+fix+forget` 28, `long+fix+filter` 28; the forgetting map at 0.001 **27**, at 0.003 **27**, at 0.01 **24**, at 0.03 **17**; the filtered sticky map at 0.0001 **13**, at 0.0003 **9**, at 0.001 **4**. By kind the designated arm (the forgetting map at 0.01) reaches 7, 10 and 7 of 10 against `exact`'s 10, 9 and 10: the bar (80% of exact) is held in hard-gaps and **missed in hard-islands and hard-current at DEV**, which would be NOT SUPPORTED on this sample. Two of the printed judgements are arithmetic of the sample, not findings: **F5** (`all@0.001+filter` at least 60 fewer than the forgetting map at 0.001) asks for a difference of 60 flights and a set of 30 cannot show more than 30, and **F7** (a drop of 15 between two filtered arms) is 13 against 9 here, a difference of 4 that a sample this size cannot read. **F6 is MISSED for a reason that is not the sample's: the filtered sticky map at 0.0001, about one phantom cell every six pings, reaches 13 of 30 (43%) against the 80% of exact registered**, so the cliff I expected "between 0.0001 and 0.001" for the sticky map is, if DEV is a fair look, below the lowest rate flown. F1, F2, F3, F4 and F8 are HELD at DEV. The registration stands as written; HELD11 is flown next, once, by `node tools/ship/subSensedFollowStudy.mjs --study falserate --set held11 --write`.

## RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 6 (2026-11-02; `node tools/ship/subSensedFollowStudy.mjs --study falserate --set held11 --write`, HELD11 = seeds 1101 to 1160 of each of hard-islands, hard-gaps and hard-current, read once; every flight is in `brain/subSensedFalseRateHeld11.jsonl`, 1,800 records): **NOT SUPPORTED** -- the forgetting map at a false-return rate ten times follow-up 4's (0.01) reaches 122 of 180 against the exact map's 175 and holds the registered bar (80% of exact) in ONE kind of three, hard-gaps. **Seven of the eight predictions held; F6 missed.** The answer to the two questions: the forgetting map has a margin of about three times follow-up 4's rate and not ten; and a filtered fix does not rescue a sticky map at any rate flown, down to one tenth of follow-up 4's.

The study command was gated and sabotaged first (81 mutants: 76 red by name, 5 caught by not finishing; the module's 40, all red by name; both in the gates' headers) and DEV was flown once (Amendment 1, which had said the designated arm missed the bar in two kinds and F6 missed, on 30 flights). Reached, of 60 a kind and 180 together:

| arm | hard-islands | hard-gaps | hard-current | three kinds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `exact` (the ceiling) | 59 | 60 | 56 | **175** (97%) |
| `long+fix+forget` (clean) | 60 | 57 | 58 | 175 |
| `long+fix+filter` (clean, sticky) | 58 | 60 | 59 | 177 |
| forgetting map, false rate **0.001** (follow-up 4's) | 55 (93% of exact) | 60 | 50 (89%) | 165 (92%) |
| forgetting map, **0.003** | 50 (85%) | 59 | 45 (**80%**) | 154 (86%) |
| forgetting map, **0.01** (designated) | **34 (58%)** | 58 (97%) | **30 (54%)** | **122** (68%) |
| forgetting map, **0.03** | 38 (64%) | 55 (92%) | 23 (41%) | 116 (64%) |
| filtered sticky map, false rate **0.0001** | 38 | 33 | 36 | **107** (59%) |
| filtered sticky map, **0.0003** | 21 | 23 | 20 | 64 (36%) |
| filtered sticky map, **0.001** | 17 | 16 | 12 | **45** (25%) |

**The bar.** The designated arm against `exact` in each kind: 34 of 59 (58%), 58 of 60 (97%) and 30 of 56 (54%), against the registered 80%: held in hard-gaps only, so **NOT SUPPORTED**. Its 60 failures are 45 collisions, 10 exits and 3 time-outs.

**The predictions.** F1 HELD: the forgetting map at 0.001 holds the bar in every kind (93%, 100%, 89%). F2 HELD: at 0.003 it still does, **by a hair** (85%, 98%, and 80.4% in hard-current: 45 of 56). F3 HELD: at 0.03 it misses the bar in two kinds (64%, 41%). F4 HELD: the counts fall at every step (165, 154, 122, 116; no rise). F5 HELD: the filtered sticky map at 0.001 reaches 120 fewer than the forgetting map at the same rate (45 against 165; registered: 60). F7 HELD: the filtered sticky map at 0.0003 reaches 43 fewer than at 0.0001 (64 against 107; registered: 15). F8 HELD: at 0.001 the forgetting map beats the filtered sticky one in every kind (55 / 60 / 50 against 17 / 16 / 12). **F6 MISSED: the filtered sticky map at 0.0001, about one phantom cell every six pings, reaches 107 of 180, 61% of the exact map's 175, against the 80% registered.** Amendment 1 had said so on DEV; the cliff I put "between 0.0001 and 0.001" for a sticky map is below the lowest rate flown.

**What the sweep shows.** (1) **The forgetting map's margin is about threefold, not tenfold, and it is not free even at the follow-up 4 rate.** The counts fall 165, 154, 122, 116 from 0.001 to 0.03: the step from 0.003 to 0.01 is where the bar is lost (154 to 122, +7 / +39, p below 0.0001) and the step beyond it is not distinguishable from nothing (116 against 122, +21 / +27, p 0.47). At follow-up 4's own rate the forgetting map is 10 flights behind the clean one here (165 against 175, +4 / +14, p 0.03; hard-current +1 / +9, p 0.02), where follow-up 4's HELD9 had it 3 behind: "harmless" was a statement about a sample of that size, and 3 to 10 flights is the cost. (2) **What breaks is collisions with the islands and the current's obstacles, not the gaps.** The failures are collisions (14, 24, 45 and 56 of 180 from 0.001 to 0.03); the median least clearance falls from 11.1 m to 10.7, 7.9 and 4.7 m, and the 3 m rung of the margin ladder is the last one in 2, 3, 13 and 36 of the 180 flights: the phantom cells close the lanes and the plan runs out of margin, as it did for follow-up 4's sticky map. Hard-gaps is almost untouched (97% at 0.01 and 92% at 0.03, the 24 m gaps 19 and 17 of 20); I did not test why. (3) **The sticky map, with its fix filtered, is dead at every rate flown.** It reaches 107, 64 and 45 at 0.0001, 0.0003 and 0.001; it loses 70 flights to the clean filtered arm (177) already at 0.0001 (+1 / +71, p below 0.0001), the 24 m gaps are 4 of 20 (the clean arm 20 of 20), and its failures are time-outs and exits as well as collisions (19 time-outs and 8 exits at 0.0001). **A forgetting map at 0.03, 300 times that rate, reaches about what the sticky one reaches at 0.0001** (116 against 107, +47 / +38, p 0.39).

**Post hoc paired tests (after HELD11 was read; not registered; exact two-sided sign tests on flights paired by scenario, `+a / +b` is the flights only the first arm reached and only the second; uncorrected for the many comparisons, descriptive only).** The clean forgetting map against `exact` +4 / +4 (p 1.0), the clean filtered sticky map against `exact` +4 / +2 (p 0.69), the two clean cures against each other +3 / +5 (p 0.73). The forgetting map at 0.001 against the clean one +4 / +14 (p 0.031); at 0.003 against 0.001 +5 / +16 (p 0.027), against the clean one +4 / +25 (p 0.0001), against `exact` +1 / +22 (p below 0.0001); at 0.01 against 0.003 +7 / +39 (p below 0.0001), against `exact` +0 / +53 (hard-islands +0 / +25, hard-current +0 / +26, both p below 0.0001; hard-gaps +0 / +2, p 0.5); at 0.03 against 0.01 +21 / +27 (p 0.47). The filtered sticky map at 0.0001 against the clean one +1 / +71, against the forgetting map at 0.001 +5 / +63; at 0.0003 against 0.0001 +12 / +55; at 0.001 against 0.0003 +21 / +40 (p 0.02), against the forgetting map at the same rate +2 / +122 (all p below 0.0001 except where given).

**What this says.** (1) The follow-up 4 result stands at its own rate, with a cost: the realistic forgetting vessel is within the bar at 0.001 and 0.003 and not at 0.01. A real sonar whose false rate is within a factor of three of a thousandth of a cell a ping is within what was tested; one a factor of ten above it is not. (2) The sticky map, with or without a filtered fix, is not a candidate once false returns exist at any rate this study can see: forgetting is the cure, and it is a partial one. (3) The cure that remains to be tried is on the marking side: a cell marked only after it reads occupied at several consecutive pings kills a white false return (it has to repeat, at a chance of the rate squared) at the price of a ping or two of delay in marking a real obstacle; a persistent fault (follow-up 5) repeats and a single ping's dropout in the other direction needs the opposite rule (a cell cleared only after several free readings). Both rules are one filter, and neither has been flown.

**What this would not say.** That 0.01 or 0.03 is the real sonar's rate (the sweep has four rates in steps of about three, coarse, and one value of the other three faults); that false returns are white (follow-up 5 made them last ten pings at 0.001 with the range law and the forgetting map lost about 6 flights to them alone; they also cluster in space on a thermocline or a school of fish); that the sticky map's cliff is below 0.0001 and not somewhere I did not look (the lowest rate flown is the one it fails at); or that hard-gaps's near-immunity is anything but what was measured. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**What it leaves, registered in `tools/ship/nextRounds.mjs`:** `nav-sensed-confirm-returns` (mark a cell only after k consecutive occupied readings, k = 2, 3, with the forgetting map and, with `nav-sensed-dropout-cure`'s clearing rule, as one detector, against the white false returns of this round and the persistent faults of follow-up 5).

## FOLLOW-UP 7: A DETECTOR FOR THE MAP (Keith, 2026-11-02: "lets do nav-sensed-dropout-cure and nav-sensed-confirm-returns"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN)

The two backlog entries are the two halves of one thing. Follow-up 5 found the forgetting map's weakness in **dropouts**: an obstacle that reads free for ten pings is taken at its word and the vessel steers into it (`objp+forget` 12 flights behind the clean map). Follow-up 6 found it in **false returns**: a cell is marked on the first ping that reads it occupied, and at a false rate of 0.01 the map reaches 68% of the exact map, the bar lost between 0.003 and 0.01. The map's rule is the same at both ends, **one reading decides**: a cell is marked after ONE occupied reading and cleared after ONE free one. This round replaces it with a **detector** with a rule at each end, tested **separately and together**:
* **Confirm (`km`)**: a free cell is marked occupied only when it has read occupied at `km` consecutive pings (the cell ensonified each time). A white false return has to repeat, at about the rate to the power `km`: 0.0001 for a rate of 0.01 and `km` = 2.
* **Hold (`kc`)**: a marked cell is cleared only when it has read free at `kc` consecutive pings. A dropout of ten pings is covered by a hold of twelve.
`(1, 1)` is the map that forgets (every earlier result's best map) and `(1, infinity)` the sticky map.

**The tension, said before the data.** A long hold is what the sticky map did, and what the sticky map costs is the fix smear (follow-up 2: the union of every ping's placements of a wall, each shifted by a different fix error, thickens it); the map forgets to cure that. A hold of twelve brings a twelve-ping union of placements back. A confirmation delays the marking of a real obstacle by a ping or two, and with the sonar's 20% misses a cell needs two hits in a row (64%). And **the hold of twelve is chosen because the dropout is ten pings: it is the cure tuned to the disease**, flown as a possibility, not as a robust design; a real dropout's length is not known.

**Two conditions, the two that broke the map** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 6: the 200 m sonar, the white 3 m fix, the margin ladder):
* **`W`**: follow-up 4's four white faults (misses 0.2, first surfaces only, a white 2 degree heading error) at a false-return rate of **0.01**, the rate at which the forgetting map failed (122 of 180 in follow-up 6).
* **`P`**: follow-up 5's `pall`, the five persistent faults together (a bias, whole-obstacle dropouts of ten pings, persistent false returns, the range law, first surfaces only).

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and a map with a detector): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; the designated detector **`d2-12`** (`km` = 2, `kc` = 12) on a sea with NO faults (`d2-12@clean`); and under each condition the six detectors `d1-1` (the incumbent, flown through the detector code to check it is the map that forgets), `d2-1` (confirm only), `d1-12` (hold only), `d2-3`, **`d2-12`** and `d3-12`: `d1-1@W`, `d2-1@W`, `d1-12@W`, `d2-3@W`, `d2-12@W`, `d3-12@W` and the same six `@P`. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights.

**One study, one new set, nothing else changed. The set is HELD12 = seeds 1201 to 1260 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated detector `d2-12` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under EACH condition**: six cells, three kinds under `W` and three under `P`. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (The incumbent already holds the three `P` cells in follow-up 5 and fails two of the three `W` cells in follow-up 6: a detector has to repair `W` without breaking `P`.)

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **G1** confirmation repairs white false returns: `d2-1@W` reaches at least 20 more than `d1-1@W`. (A false return has to repeat: 17 phantom cells a ping become about 0.2.)
* **G2** and does nothing for dropouts: `d2-1@P` is within 10 of `d1-1@P`. (A dropout makes cells read free, which a confirmation rule does not look at.)
* **G3** a hold alone makes white false returns far worse: `d1-12@W` reaches at least 20 fewer than `d1-1@W`. (Each phantom now lasts twelve pings instead of one: some two hundred on the map at once, against fifty at a rate of 0.03.)
* **G4** a hold alone helps with dropouts: `d1-12@P` reaches more than `d1-1@P`. (The hold covers the ten pings; against it the fix smear a hold brings back.)
* **G5** together they repair the white false returns: `d2-12` holds the bar in all three kinds under `W`.
* **G6** and keep the dropout cure: `d2-12` holds the bar in all three kinds under `P`.
* **G7** and cost little on a sea with no faults: `d2-12@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`. (A marking delayed by a ping; against it the twelve-ping union of the fix's placements.)
* **G8** the two rules are better together than either alone: summed over the two conditions, `d2-12` reaches more than `d2-1` and more than `d1-12`.
`d2-3` and `d3-12` are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That these are the right numbers: `km` of 2 and 3 and `kc` of 3 and 12 are six points on a plane (an M-of-N rule, a range-gated clear or a median over the last pings are untried), and the hold is tuned to the dropout the study throws at it. The faults are the two sets that broke the map and not a sample of faults; a detector tuned on them is tested on them. The persistent false returns of `P` are single cells; real ones cluster. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 7 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the module and the study command were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD12 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The module gate (69 mutants, 66 red by name, 3 equivalent, logged in `subSensedNav-selfcheck.mjs`), the study command and gate (`--study detector`; 82 mutants, 75 red by name, 5 caught by not finishing, 2 equivalent, logged as Addendum 5 in `subSensedFollowStudy-selfcheck.mjs`; the gate passes with all checks), and the DEV set (seeds 1 to 10 of each kind, 30 scenarios, 15 arms, 450 flights; `--set dev` writes nothing). The study command and the module were not edited after their sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 30):** `exact` 29, the clean `long+fix+forget` 28, `d2-12@clean` 27; under `W`: `d1-1` 24, `d2-1` 28, `d1-12` **3**, `d2-3` 28, `d2-12` 23, `d3-12` 25; under `P`: `d1-1` 26, `d2-1` 28, `d1-12` 26, `d2-3` 25, `d2-12` 29, `d3-12` 28. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds (20 flights, 10, 8, the bar) are made for 180 flights and cannot be met or missed meaningfully on 30, so the DEV verdict (PARTLY SUPPORTED) and its G1 to G8 lines say nothing about HELD12.

**What is worth saying before the set is flown, and what is not changed by it.**
* *The plumbing is right.* The incumbent flown through the detector code (`d1-1`) reproduces the map that forgets (24 of 30 under `W` is 80%, follow-up 6 reached 68% at 0.01 on 180); `d2-12@clean` is within one flight of the clean map; no arm crashed, timed out wholesale or wrote a file.
* *`d1-12@W` is 3 of 30, and the way it fails is not the way the registration said.* G3's reasoning was that every phantom lasts twelve pings, so "some two hundred on the map at once"; the registration did not say what the vessel would DO with a sea of phantoms. DEV says it **collides** (27 of 30 collided, none timed out, median clearance -0.02 m, 52% of the grid ensonified): when the phantoms close every route the plan has none, and the flight goes into the true obstacles. That is a finding about the mechanism (a map full of phantoms is not a map that holds the vessel at the quay; it is one that has no plan), not a change to G3, which asks for 20 fewer and is, on DEV, 21 of 30.
* *`d2-12@W` is below `d2-1@W` and `d2-3@W` on DEV (23 against 28 and 28), with three timeouts.* Not read as a result (30 flights), and flagged here so that it is not found afterwards and called a surprise: if it is there on HELD12, it will be the first thing the post hoc looks at (a hold of twelve behind a mark of two keeps the phantoms that DO pass the confirmation twelve pings, and `W` is where they pass).

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `d2-12`, the bar, the verdict rule, G1 to G8 with their reasons, the set (HELD12 = seeds 1201 to 1260 of each of the three kinds) and the single reading of it are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 7 (2026-11-03; HELD12 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetectorHeld12.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number after "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: PARTLY SUPPORTED (4 of 6 cells).** The designated detector `d2-12` (a run of two to mark, twelve free readings to clear) reaches 80% of the exact map's count in each kind in **four of the six cells**: under `W` in `hard-gaps` only (islands 46 against the 48 the bar needs of exact's 59, current 47 against 48 of exact's 60), under `P` in all three. **Six of eight predictions held; G5 and G8 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 177 (59/58/60); the clean `long+fix+forget` 176 (59/59/58); `d2-12@clean` 171 (58/60/53). Under `W`: `d1-1` 128 (35/57/36), `d2-1` 163 (56/59/48), `d1-12` **56** (22/15/19), `d2-3` 163 (55/56/52), **`d2-12` 149 (46/56/47)**, `d3-12` 157 (54/53/50). Under `P`: `d1-1` 159 (56/49/54), `d2-1` 150 (54/46/50), `d1-12` 160 (53/55/52), `d2-3` 150 (55/44/51), **`d2-12` 162 (53/57/52)**, `d3-12` 157 (55/52/50).

**The eight predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **G1 HELD**: confirmation repairs the white false returns: `d2-1@W` 163 against `d1-1@W` 128 (+35, asked 20).
* **G2 HELD**: and does nothing for dropouts: `d2-1@P` 150 against `d1-1@P` 159 (9 apart, asked within 10: **held by one flight**; it is nine fewer, not zero).
* **G3 HELD**: a hold alone makes white false returns far worse: `d1-12@W` 56 against 128 (72 fewer, asked 20). It collides (118 of 180 collided, median clearance -0.01 m): the sea of phantoms leaves the plan no route and the flight goes into the true obstacles, as DEV had shown (Amendment 1).
* **G4 HELD, by one flight**: a hold alone helps with dropouts: `d1-12@P` 160 against `d1-1@P` 159. **Not evidence of anything pooled**; the help is in the narrowest gaps (below).
* **G5 MISSED**: together they repair the white false returns: `d2-12` holds the bar in **one** of three kinds under `W` (gaps).
* **G6 HELD**: and keep the dropout cure: `d2-12` holds the bar in all three kinds under `P`.
* **G7 HELD**: and cost little on a sea with no faults: `d2-12@clean` 171 against 176 (5 fewer, asked within 8).
* **G8 MISSED**: together they beat either rule alone, summed over `W` and `P`: `d2-12` 311 against `d2-1` **313** and `d1-12` 216. The registered reason ("the pair is better than either alone") was wrong in the form stated: **the two rules are better in different conditions, not together.**

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The confirmation is the false-return cure and the hold is not.** Under `W` the confirmation alone (`d2-1` and `d2-3`, both 163 of 180 and 3 of 3 cells) does what the pair was supposed to and more; adding the hold of twelve to it LOSES 14 flights (`d2-12` 149; **8 timeouts**, against none for `d2-1`). Amendment 1 flagged that DEV showed this ("`d2-12@W` below `d2-1@W` ... will be the first thing the post hoc looks at"): it is there.
* **The hold is a cure for the narrowest gaps and nothing else.** Under `P` in `hard-gaps` by width, 24 m: `exact` 18 of 20, `d1-1` 10, `d2-1` 7, `d2-3` 7, `d1-12` 15, **`d2-12` 18**, `d3-12` 13; at 32 m and 40 m every arm is at 19 or 20 except `d2-3` (17 at 32 m). **A confirmation alone is worse than forgetting at 24 m under dropouts (7 against 10): it delays marking, and the dropout takes what was not yet marked** (follow-up 16's tension, seen here without the hold).
* **On the registered bar the designated detector ties the incumbent.** POST HOC, the six-cell count of every detector flown: `d1-1` 4, `d2-1` 5, `d1-12` 3, `d2-3` 5, **`d2-12` 4**, **`d3-12` 6**. The map that forgets holds all three `P` cells and one `W` cell (4 of 6); `d2-12` holds all three `P` cells and one `W` cell (4 of 6). It repairs `W` against the incumbent (post hoc sign test, pooled over the kinds, `d2-12@W` +37 / `d1-1@W` +16, p 0.0055) without breaking `P`, but not far enough to hold the bar.
* **POST HOC and flagged as a selection: `d3-12` (a run of three to mark, twelve to clear) holds all six cells** (`W` 54/53/50, `P` 55/52/50 against 48/46.4/48 needed). It was one of six arms flown and not the designated, and it is named here *after* the set was read: **the verdict stays PARTLY SUPPORTED**, `d3-12` is not a finding, and a stricter confirmation with the same hold is the candidate for a later registration on a fresh set (follow-ups 8 to 23 were registered BEFORE this set was flown, with their own designated arms, and none of them moves).
* **POST HOC, the hold's cost on a clean sea sits in the current.** `d2-12@clean` in `hard-current` is 53 of 60 against 58 for the clean map (3 left the arena, 2 timed out); in the other two kinds it is 58 and 60 against 59 and 59. One kind, 5 flights, not tested.
* **POST HOC, the replans halve.** Median plans per flight under `W`: `d1-1` 133, `d2-1` 83, `d2-12` 63, `d3-12` 37 (the map that forgets redraws the plan every time a phantom flickers).
* **POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios):** `d2-1@W` +42 / `d1-1@W` +7 (p < 0.0001); `d1-12@W` +14 / `d1-1@W` +86 (p < 0.0001); `d2-12@W` +37 / `d1-1@W` +16 (p 0.0055); `d2-12@P` +17 / `d1-1@P` +14 (p 0.72); `d2-12@P` against `d1-1@P` in `hard-gaps` alone +11 / +3 (p 0.057). `d2-12@clean` +3 / clean map +8 (p 0.23).

**What it does not say.** That `d2-12` is the wrong detector: it is one point of the plane and the bar is 80% of exact in six cells, two of which it missed by 2 and 1 flights; that the hold hurts everywhere (it helps 24 m gaps under dropouts by 8 flights of 20); that `d3-12` is the better one (a selection after the fact). The faults are the two sets that broke the map, and the dropout is the one length the hold of twelve was chosen for (the registration said so: *"the cure tuned to the disease"*); follow-ups 8 to 23, registered before this set was flown, take the length, the range and the rules apart. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 8: THE HOLD THAT KNOWS HOW FAR (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-dropout-range"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-up 7's set (HELD12) was still unflown, so nothing below was shaped by its result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** There was no backlog entry of that name. The nearest thing is the range idea left in `nav-sensed-dropout-cure`: *keep an obstacle that was seen close in as occupied*. It is taken here in both senses of "range" the dropout has, which the one study can carry: **how far** (the hold depends on the range of the cell) and **how long** (the dropout's length is swept). If the name meant only the length sweep, that is in this study too (conditions `D3`, `D10`, `D30`); if it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question.** Follow-up 7's detector holds a marked cell for `kc` free readings, the same everywhere. A dropout is dangerous where the vessel is close to the obstacle (the collisions of follow-up 5 were at close range, in the 24 m gaps), and the dropout's rate in follow-up 5's `pall` falls with range (a fault at 200 m is common, one at 60 m rare). A hold that is long where it matters and short where it does not would keep the near cells through a dropout and let the far cells be forgotten freely, which is what keeps the fix smear (the thick-wall union of a long hold's placements) and a far phantom from lasting. Does it do better than the uniform hold, and across how long a dropout?

**The rule** (the detector of follow-up 7 with one more number): `clear` free readings clear a marked cell that is **farther than `near`** from the sonar at the ping; `clearNear` free readings clear one that is **within `near`** (inclusive; the range is from the believed position to the cell's centre, at that ping). Marking is as before (`mark` consecutive occupied readings). With `clearNear` equal to `clear` it is follow-up 7's detector.

**Conditions.** Whole-obstacle dropouts only, **with the range law** (the chance a dropout starts is 0.2 x 2 x (range / 200 m) squared of the follow-up 5 rate, the long-run share of time in fault 0.2 averaged over the sector, as `pall`'s obstacles), and three lengths: **`D3`** (3 pings), **`D10`** (10, follow-up 5's) and **`D30`** (30 pings, 90 m of travel). **`W`**: follow-up 4's four white faults at a false rate of 0.01 (follow-up 7's `W`), to see the false-return cure survive. And a sea with no faults. Everything else as follow-ups 3 to 7.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; and three detectors, **`inc`** (1, 1: the map that forgets), **`uni`** (mark 2, clear 12 everywhere: follow-up 7's designated) and **`lock`** (**mark 2, clear 1, clearNear 30, near 80 m**: the designated, a hold of thirty pings, 90 m of travel, on cells within 80 m, a single free reading beyond): `inc`, `uni` and `lock` under `D3`, `D10` and `D30` (9 arms), under `W` (3 arms), and `lock` on a sea with no faults (`lock@clean`). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD13 = seeds 1301 to 1360 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `lock` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `D30` and under `W`**: six cells (the longest dropout, and the false returns that broke the incumbent). **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** A long hold near the vessel is a sticky map near the vessel, and what the sticky map cost was the fix smear: the thickened wall closes a 24 m gap. A near lock may buy back dropouts and lose the gaps. And a hold longer than the thing it covers is a hold that keeps a phantom longer than it has to: under `W` a near false return is held thirty pings.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **H1** a longer dropout hurts the map that forgets: `inc@D30` reaches at least 10 fewer than `inc@D3`. (A dropout of 30 pings covers the whole near approach; one of 3 hardly any of it.)
* **H2** a hold of twelve covers a dropout of ten: `uni@D10` reaches at least 5 more than `inc@D10`.
* **H3** and not one of thirty: `uni@D30` reaches at least 5 fewer than `uni@D3`. (The hold ends at twelve pings and the dropout does not.)
* **H4** the near lock covers it: `lock@D30` reaches at least 10 more than `uni@D30`.
* **H5** and does not give up the short ones: `lock@D3` is within 8 of `uni@D3` and `lock@D10` is within 8 of `uni@D10`.
* **H6** and keeps the false-return cure: `lock@W` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind.
* **H7** and costs little on a sea with no faults: `lock@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **H8** summed over `D3`, `D10` and `D30`, `lock` reaches more than `inc` and more than `uni`.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, a thirty-ping hold, a confirmation of two and a far hold of one are the right numbers (one point of the plane, as follow-up 7's six): a lock that depends on the cell's range at each ping, not at the ping it was marked, is one choice among several (the range at marking, the nearest range at which it has read occupied). The dropouts are whole obstacles and not partial shadows; their lengths are fixed per condition; the range law is a square. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 8 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD13 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@D3` 14/18; `uni@D3` 17/18; `lock@D3` 16/18; `inc@D10` 16/18; `uni@D10` 17/18; `lock@D10` 15/18; `inc@D30` 15/18; `uni@D30` 16/18; `lock@D30` 13/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `uni@W` 12/18; `lock@W` 15/18; `lock@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `lock`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD13 = seeds 1301 to 1360 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 8 (2026-11-03; HELD13 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDropoutRangeHeld13.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (2 of 6 cells).** The designated `lock` against 80% of exact's count: D30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current HELD; W: islands MISSED, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **4 of 8 predictions held; H4, H5, H6, H8 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 175 (60/59/56); `long+fix+forget` 168 (56/56/56); `inc@D3` 161 (57/53/51); `uni@D3` 168 (58/58/52); `lock@D3` 154 (54/49/51); `inc@D10` 157 (56/50/51); `uni@D10` 169 (59/59/51); `lock@D10` 145 (52/43/50); `inc@D30` 145 (54/44/47); `uni@D30` 148 (55/46/47); `lock@D30` 134 (50/37/47); `inc@W` 129 (36/57/36); `uni@W` 149 (50/58/41); `lock@W` 134 (47/47/40); `lock@clean` 166 (54/60/52).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **H1 HELD**: a longer dropout hurts the map that forgets: inc@D30 reaches at least 10 fewer than inc@D3. inc@D3 161, inc@D30 145: +16 against the 10 asked.
* **H2 HELD**: a hold of twelve covers a dropout of ten: uni@D10 reaches at least 5 more than inc@D10. uni@D10 169, inc@D10 157: +12 against the 5 asked.
* **H3 HELD**: and not one of thirty: uni@D30 reaches at least 5 fewer than uni@D3. uni@D3 168, uni@D30 148: +20 against the 5 asked.
* **H4 MISSED**: the near lock covers it: lock@D30 reaches at least 10 more than uni@D30. lock@D30 134, uni@D30 148: -14 against the 10 asked.
* **H5 MISSED**: and does not give up the short ones: lock@D3 is within 8 of uni@D3 and lock@D10 is within 8 of uni@D10. lock@D3 154, uni@D3 168: 14 apart, within 8 asked ; lock@D10 145, uni@D10 169: 24 apart, within 8 asked.
* **H6 MISSED**: and keeps the false-return cure: lock@W holds the bar in every kind. lock@W: 47/48.0 47/47.2 40/44.8 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **H7 HELD**: and costs little on a sea with no faults: lock@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. lock@clean 166, long+fix+forget 168: 2 apart, within 8 asked.
* **H8 MISSED**: summed over D3, D10 and D30, lock reaches more than inc and more than uni. lock@D3+lock@D10+lock@D30 433 against inc@D3+inc@D10+inc@D30 463 ; lock@D3+lock@D10+lock@D30 433 against uni@D3+uni@D10+uni@D30 485.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The near lock does not cover the dropout, it makes it worse.** The designated `lock` (mark 2, one free reading beyond 80 m, thirty within) holds **2 of the six bar cells** (islands and current under D30). It is **below the map that forgets** at every dropout length (D3 154 against `inc` 161, D10 145 against 157, D30 134 against 145) and **below follow-up 7's uniform hold of twelve** (`uni`: 168, 169, 148) by 14, 24 and 14. H4 (the lock covers the long dropout, +10 over `uni`) missed by 24 in the wrong direction, H5 (it keeps the short ones) missed, H8 (summed over D3, D10 and D30, `lock` above `inc` and `uni`) missed: 433 for `lock`, 463 for `inc`, 485 for `uni` (of 540).
* **The hold of twelve, uniform, is the best of the three, and the registered cure holds.** H1 held (`inc` loses 16 from D3 to D30: 161 to 145), H2 held (`uni` +12 over `inc` at D10: 169 against 157; it is above the clean map's 168), H3 held (`uni` loses 20 from D3 to D30: the hold of twelve ends before a dropout of thirty does). `uni` holds 4 of the six bar cells and `inc` 3. Under white false returns `uni` is 20 above `inc` (149 against 129) and `lock` 5 above (134).
* **The cost of the lock is in the narrow gaps and in the plan's churn.** The 24 m gaps reached, `inc` / `uni` / `lock`: D3 13 / 19 / 13, D10 13 / 20 / 10, D30 10 / 14 / 7, W 19 / 19 / 10 (exact 19 of 20). `lock` collided in 22, 28 and 44 flights of 180 under D3, D10 and D30 against 9, 8 and 30 for `uni`, and replanned 78 times a flight against 48 to 54 for `uni` (mean marks and clears per flight, `lock@D3`: 1,224 and 931; `uni@D3`: 395 and 19): a likely reason, not isolated by any arm here, is that a cell is held thirty readings once it is within 80 m and cleared at the first free reading beyond it, so the cells at the 80 m line flip between the two and the plan is rebuilt each time.
* **Why the lock cannot cover a dropout that begins far away (the registered tension; consistent with the numbers, not isolated by an arm).** The lock keeps a cell only while it is within 80 m at the ping. A dropout of 30 pings is 90 m of travel; one that begins at 150 m takes the far cells at once (a hold of one) and the wall is gone from the map until the dropout ends, 60 m from it. POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios): `lock` against `uni` D3 +4 / +18 (p 0.0043), D10 +3 / +27 (p < 0.0001), D30 +6 / +20 (p 0.0094), W +14 / +29 (p 0.032); `lock` against `inc` D3 +8 / +15 (p 0.21), D10 +8 / +20 (p 0.036), D30 +8 / +19 (p 0.052).
* **Nothing is lost on a sea with no faults.** H7 held: `lock@clean` 166 against the clean map's 168 (POST HOC +7 / +9, p 0.80). The cost of the lock is a cost of the faults.

**What it does not say.** That a range-aware hold cannot work: this is one lock (thirty within 80 m, one beyond, gated by the range at the ping) and follow-ups 13 to 31 vary the evidence, the range the gate goes by and the clear. That the uniform hold of twelve is a good detector: it is a cure tuned to a dropout of ten, and it loses 20 flights from D3 to D30. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 9: THE CONFIRMATION THAT KNOWS HOW FAR (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-confirm-range"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 and 8 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is the mirror of follow-up 8: there the *hold* knew how far, here the *confirmation* does. Taken in both senses of "range": **how far** (the number of consecutive occupied readings a cell needs before it is marked depends on its distance from the sonar) and **how wide a range of false-return rates** (the sweep of the false rate, with the range law and without it). If it meant only the sweep, that is in this study too; if it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question.** Follow-up 7's confirmation (`mark` 2) delays marking a REAL obstacle by a ping everywhere, and the false returns it is bought against are not equally likely everywhere: where noise grows with range (follow-up 5's range law, rates 2 x (range / 200 m) squared times the mean) a cell at 200 m is a hundred times likelier to be a false return than one at 20 m. A confirmation that asks for more repeats far away, where false returns are common and the vessel has time, and marks at the first reading near the vessel, where a delay costs a collision and false returns are rare, might beat a uniform one. It is a **bet on the range law**: if the false rate does not fall near the vessel, the near zone is unprotected.

**The rule** (the detector of follow-up 7 with a second `mark`): `mark` consecutive occupied readings mark a free cell **farther than `near`** from the sonar at the ping; `markNear` mark one **within `near`** (inclusive; the range from the believed position to the cell's centre, at that ping); `clear` is as before. With `markNear` equal to `mark` it is follow-up 7's detector.

**Conditions.** Follow-up 4's four white faults (misses, first surfaces only, a 2 degree white heading error) with the false-return rate: **`R1`** and **`R3`**, the **range law** on (the misses and the false returns both grow as 2 x (range / 200 m) squared of their mean, which is 0.2 for misses and **0.01** or **0.03** for false returns; at 200 m a false return is 0.02 or 0.06 a cell a ping, at 60 m 0.0018 or 0.0054); **`W`**, follow-up 7's: the false rate **uniform** at 0.01 (the law NOT holding); and a sea with no faults. Everything else as follow-ups 3 to 8.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and a clear of 1, so the confirmation is the only thing that varies): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; **`inc`** (mark 1: the map that forgets), **`c2`** (mark 2 everywhere), **`c3`** (mark 3 everywhere) and **`cr`** (**mark 3 beyond 80 m, markNear 1 within 80 m**: the designated) under `R1` and `R3` (8 arms); `inc`, `c2` and `cr` under `W` (3 arms); and `cr@clean`. Fourteen arms, 2,520 flights. **The set is HELD14 = seeds 1401 to 1460 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `cr` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3` and under `W`**: six cells (the range law at its harshest, and the uniform false rate where the law does not hold). **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** A confirmation of three is three readings in a row, and with the sonar's misses (0.2, up to 0.4 far away under the range law) a real far obstacle is read three times running about a fifth of the time at 200 m: the far part of the long sonar's reach is thinned, and the long sonar was bought for the gaps. And `cr` within 80 m is the map that forgets, on the cells that matter most.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **K1** a range-aware confirmation repairs the range-law false returns: `cr@R3` reaches at least 15 more than `inc@R3`. (Far phantoms need three repeats, 0.06 cubed; the near ones are the rare ones.)
* **K2** and is as good as a uniform confirmation of two there: `cr@R3` is within 10 of `c2@R3`. (A third reading far away against no delay near.)
* **K3** a third reading is not worth it everywhere: `c3@R3` is within 10 of `c2@R3`. (The second reading kills most of the phantoms; the third costs a streak of three against the sonar's misses.)
* **K4** the range-aware confirmation is a bet on the law: under the uniform false rate `cr@W` reaches at least 10 fewer than `c2@W`. (The near zone of 270 cells has 2.7 phantoms a ping at 0.01, marked at the first reading, in the place the vessel hits them.)
* **K5** it holds the bar in every kind under the range law: `cr@R3`.
* **K6** and does NOT hold it in every kind under the uniform rate: `cr@W` misses the bar in at least one kind (the follow-up 6 failure of the map that forgets, on the near zone).
* **K7** it costs little on a sea with no faults: `cr@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **K8** summed over `R1` and `R3`, `cr` reaches more than `inc` and more than `c3`.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, a far confirmation of three and a near one of one are the right numbers; that false returns follow a square law (the law is the lever the whole idea pulls); that a real sonar's noise falls near the vessel (it does for a spreading loss, but not for a reverberation floor). Only the confirmation is varied: the hold of follow-up 7 and the near lock of follow-up 8 are not combined with it, and a detector that has both a range-dependent mark and a range-dependent clear is the obvious next table. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (2 of 6 cells).** The designated `cr` against 80% of exact's count: R3: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current HELD; W: islands MISSED, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **4 of 8 predictions held; K1, K2, K5, K8 missed.**

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**

* **K2 MISSED**: and is as good as a uniform confirmation of two there: cr@R3 is within 10 of c2@R3. cr@R3 123, c2@R3 161: 38 apart, within 10 asked.

* **K5 MISSED**: it holds the bar in every kind under the range law: cr@R3. cr@R3: 48/48.0 25/48.0 50/45.6 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **K6 HELD**: and does NOT hold it in every kind under the uniform rate: cr@W misses the bar in at least one kind. cr@W: 38/48.0 18/48.0 24/45.6 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **K7 HELD**: it costs little on a sea with no faults: cr@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. cr@clean 165, long+fix+forget 171: 6 apart, within 8 asked.
* **K8 MISSED**: summed over R1 and R3, cr reaches more than inc and more than c3. cr@R1+cr@R3 259 against inc@R1+inc@R3 312 ; cr@R1+cr@R3 259 against c3@R1+c3@R3 306.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The designated range-aware confirmation is the WORST arm of every sea.** `cr` (a run of three beyond 80 m, one reading within) holds **2 of the six bar cells** (islands and current under R3). It is below the map that forgets, the uniform run of two and the uniform run of three in every condition flown: R1 136 against 161 / 157 / 153 (`inc` / `c2` / `c3`), R3 123 against 151 / 161 / 153, W 80 against 135 / 159 (`c3` was not flown under W). Four of eight predictions held (K3, K4, K6, K7); **K1, K2, K5 and K8 missed**, and K1 missed in the wrong direction: `cr@R3` is 28 *below* `inc@R3`, not 15 above.
* **The bet on the law is called, and the price is far larger than the 10 asked.** Under the uniform false rate `cr@W` is 79 flights below `c2@W` (80 against 159; K4 asked 10, held), collides in 84 of 180 flights with a median minimum clearance of 1.7 m (the other arms 8.0 to 9.8 m), and reaches 1 of 20 of the 24 m gaps and 7 of 20 of the 32 m gaps. POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled): `cr@W` +5 / `c2@W` +84 (p < 0.0001), `cr@W` +14 / `inc@W` +69 (p < 0.0001).
* **It is also the worst where the law holds, which the registration did not say.** The narrow gaps again: 24 m reached `inc` / `c2` / `c3` / `cr` under R1 19 / 14 / 9 / 5, under R3 19 / 15 / 8 / 1 (exact 20 of 20). POST HOC: `cr@R3` +21 / `inc@R3` +49 (p 0.0011), `cr@R3` +9 / `c2@R3` +47 (p < 0.0001), `cr@R3` +11 / `c3@R3` +41 (p < 0.0001). A mixed rule that is worse than both of its uniform parents (mark of one everywhere, mark of three everywhere) is not what the registered reasoning predicted; **no arm here isolates why**. One candidate, untested: the 80 m line itself, where a wall the vessel is closing on is marked a cell at a time (three readings at 81 m, one at 79 m) and the plan changes with each (`cr@R3` replans 125 times a flight against 63 for `c3` and 108 for `c2`).
* **The uniform run of two is the best of the family flown, and it was not the designated.** POST HOC, the six-cell tally over R3 and W: `c2` 6 (it holds every bar cell), `inc` 3, `c3` 2 (not flown under W), `cr` 2. `c2` against `inc` is the false-return cure of follow-up 7 again: 161 against 151 under R3, 159 against 135 under W. Under R1, where the rate is a third of R3's, `inc` (161) is above `c2` (157) and `c3` (153): at a mean false rate of 0.01 with the range law the confirmation does not pay on the count.
* **The cost on a sea with no faults is small and not nothing.** `cr@clean` 165 against the clean map's 171 (K7 held, 6 apart; POST HOC +8 / +14, p 0.29).

**What it does not say.** That a confirmation that depends on range cannot work: this is one rule (three beyond, one within, a step at 80 m, a clear of one) and follow-ups 10, 14, 17 to 19, 25 and 26 vary the near rule, the far rule and the hold. That the range law is false; the sea where it holds (R1, R3) is where `cr` is least bad and it is still the worst arm. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 10: THE DETECTOR THAT KNOWS HOW FAR, BOTH WAYS (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 9 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is the table follow-up 9 ended on: *a detector that has both a range-dependent mark and a range-dependent clear*. Follow-up 8 gives the HOLD a range, follow-up 9 gives the CONFIRMATION one; this round gives the one detector both, and puts it against the uniform detector of follow-up 7 across the conditions each of them was made for and the two that try them. If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The idea, in one line.** Near the vessel a reading is reliable (the range law: noise grows with the square of the range) and a mistake is deadly, so **trust it: mark at once and keep what was marked** for a long hold; far from the vessel a reading is unreliable and a mistake is cheap, so **be a sceptic: ask for repeats before marking and forget freely**.

**The rule** (the detector of follow-up 7 with two thresholds for each end): a cell **farther than `near`** from the sonar at the ping is marked after `mark` consecutive occupied readings and cleared after `clear` free ones; a cell **within `near`** (inclusive; believed position to the cell's centre, at that ping) is marked after `markNear` and cleared after `clearNear`. With the near values equal to the far ones it is follow-up 7's detector.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 9):
* **`D30`** (follow-up 8): whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law (the chance one starts is 0.2 x 2 x (range / 200 m) squared of the follow-up 5 rate).
* **`R3`** (follow-up 9): follow-up 4's four white faults with the false-return and miss rates following the range law, the false rate **0.03** at its mean (0.06 at 200 m).
* **`W`** (follow-up 7): the same faults with the false rate **uniform** at 0.01, the law NOT holding.
* **`P`** (follow-up 5): the persistent faults together (`pall`: a bias, dropouts of ten pings, persistent false returns, the range law, first surfaces only).
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; and under each of the four conditions the three detectors **`inc`** (1, 1: the map that forgets), **`uni`** (mark 2, clear 12 everywhere: follow-up 7's designated) and **`rd`** (**mark 3, markNear 1, clear 1, clearNear 30, near 80 m**: the designated) -- 12 arms -- and `rd@clean`. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD15 = seeds 1501 to 1560 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rd` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `D30` and under `R3`**: six cells, the two conditions the range-aware rules were made for. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W` and `P` are the stress: they are in the predictions, not in the verdict, because they ask what the bet on the law costs, not whether the detector works where the law holds.)

**The tensions, said before the data.** Everything that was said of the two halves holds at once. A near hold of thirty is a near sticky map and brings back the fix smear that closes a 24 m gap (follow-up 8's risk); a far confirmation of three thins the long sonar's far reach against its own misses (follow-up 9's); and the near zone is unprotected from false returns if the law fails (`W`). The question is whether the two rules cover each other: the far scepticism does what the near trust cannot (kill the far phantoms), the near hold does what the far forgetting cannot (keep the near wall through a dropout).

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **M1** range-awareness pays where the law holds: summed over `D30` and `R3`, `rd` reaches at least 10 more than `uni`. (A hold of thirty near against twelve, a confirmation of three far against two, no delay near.)
* **M2** `rd` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `D30`.
* **M3** and under `R3`.
* **M4** the bet on the law has a price: under `W`, `rd` reaches at least 10 fewer than `uni`. (The near zone marks a false return at the first reading, the place the vessel hits them.)
* **M5** under the persistent mix `P`, `rd` is within 10 of `uni` (either side). (A dropout of ten is covered by both; false returns are rarer near.)
* **M6** on a sea with no faults `rd@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **M7** `rd` reaches more than `inc` under each of the four conditions (`D30`, `R3`, `W`, `P`, each on its own).

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 3 and 1, 1 and 30 are the right numbers, or that the thresholds should depend on range in a step (a smooth function of range is untried); that the law is true of a real sonar; that the detector is better than any other (a median over the last pings, an M-of-N rule, or the filtered fix, are untried here). Four conditions and one designated detector are a sample of the plane. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 10 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD15 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@D30` 15/18; `uni@D30` 16/18; `rd@D30` 13/18; `inc@R3` 16/18; `uni@R3` 15/18; `rd@R3` 2/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `uni@W` 12/18; `rd@W` 5/18; `inc@P` 16/18; `uni@P` 18/18; `rd@P` 12/18; `rd@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**What DEV showed that the registration did not say, and is said here so that it is not found afterwards and called a surprise:** `rd@R3` reached 2 of 18, `rd@W` reached 5 of 18 (the other arms 12 to 18). It is the mechanism the registrations already name for a near hold with a mark of one or two readings (follow-up 7's `d1-12@W` was 3 of 30 on its DEV): near false returns are marked at once and held for thirty readings, some forty or fifty phantom cells sit within 80 m, the plan has no route and the vessel goes into the true obstacles. It is **not read as a result** (18 scenarios) and it moves **no prediction and no verdict rule**; it means the registered predictions and bar cells that need such an arm to reach 80% of exact are likely to be missed, and the study will say so.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `rd`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD15 = seeds 1501 to 1560 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 10 (2026-11-03; HELD15 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorRangeHeld15.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (1 of 6 cells).** The designated `rd` against 80% of exact's count: D30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current MISSED; R3: islands MISSED, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **1 of 7 predictions held; M1, M2, M3, M5, M6, M7 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 176 (60/59/57); `long+fix+forget` 170 (55/59/56); `inc@D30` 140 (53/40/47); `uni@D30` 152 (56/44/52); `rd@D30` 130 (54/33/43); `inc@R3` 146 (46/60/40); `uni@R3` 154 (48/57/49); `rd@R3` 18 (7/3/8); `inc@W` 123 (35/58/30); `uni@W` 150 (49/55/46); `rd@W` 36 (15/8/13); `inc@P` 148 (56/45/47); `uni@P` 156 (55/54/47); `rd@P` 99 (43/17/39); `rd@clean` 152 (53/55/44).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **M1 MISSED**: range-awareness pays where the law holds: summed over D30 and R3, rd reaches at least 10 more than uni. rd@D30+rd@R3 148, uni@D30+uni@R3 306: -158 against the 10 asked.
* **M2 MISSED**: rd holds the bar in every kind under D30. rd@D30: 54/48.0 33/47.2 43/45.6 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **M3 MISSED**: and under R3. rd@R3: 7/48.0 3/47.2 8/45.6 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **M4 HELD**: the bet on the law has a price: under W, rd reaches at least 10 fewer than uni. uni@W 150, rd@W 36: +114 against the 10 asked.
* **M5 MISSED**: under the persistent mix P, rd is within 10 of uni. rd@P 99, uni@P 156: 57 apart, within 10 asked.
* **M6 MISSED**: on a sea with no faults rd@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. rd@clean 152, long+fix+forget 170: 18 apart, within 8 asked.
* **M7 MISSED**: rd reaches more than inc under each of the four conditions. rd@D30 130 against inc@D30 140 ; rd@R3 18 against inc@R3 146 ; rd@W 36 against inc@W 123 ; rd@P 99 against inc@P 148.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The designated range-aware detector is the worst arm of every condition, the sea with no faults included.** `rd` (a run of three beyond 80 m and one reading within, a plain lock of thirty readings within 80 m, one free reading beyond) holds **1 of the six bar cells** (islands under D30). Its counts of 180: D30 130, R3 **18**, W **36**, P 99, and **152 on the sea with no faults** (the clean map reaches 170). Under R3 it collided in 88 flights, left the arena in 32 and timed out in 42, with a median minimum clearance of 1.25 m and 61% of the grid ensonified (82% for the clean map); under W 122 collided, the median clearance is -0.02 m and 51% of the grid was ensonified. **One of seven predictions held (M4, the price of the bet on the law, by 114 flights where 10 were asked); M1, M2, M3, M5, M6 and M7 missed.** The DEV set had shown the collapse (`rd@R3` 2 of 18, Amendment 1) and moved nothing.
* **The mechanism looks like the near zone's, as registered for the plain lock (consistent with the numbers, not isolated by an arm), and the arm costs even where there are no false returns.** Within 80 m `rd` marks at the first reading and holds a cell thirty readings; every near false return (the range law makes them rare near, not absent) and every flicker of the fix is held thirty pings, the map fills with phantoms (median occupied cells at the end under W: 256 for `rd` against 123 for `inc` and 120 for `uni`) and the plan has no route (the 24 m gaps reached under R3, W and P: 0, 0 and 2 of 20; on the clean sea 15 of 20 against the clean map's 19). That is follow-up 7's `d1-12@W` and follow-up 8's `lock` again, and a strict far rule does not rescue it: M1 (range-awareness pays, +10 over `uni` summed over D30 and R3) missed by 168 flights the wrong way (148 against 306).
* **The registered comparison arm is the good one.** `uni` (follow-up 7's detector: a run of two to mark, a hold of twelve at every range) is above `inc` in all four seas: D30 152 against 140, R3 154 against 146, W 150 against 123, P 156 against 148, and holds 11 of the 12 bar-like cells over the four conditions. POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios): `rd` against `uni` D30 +5 / +27 (p 0.0001), R3 +3 / +139 (p < 0.0001), W +2 / +116 (p < 0.0001), P +2 / +59 (p < 0.0001); `rd` against `inc` D30 +9 / +19 (p 0.087), P +8 / +57 (p < 0.0001); `rd@clean` against the clean map +5 / +23 (p 0.0009).
* **What it does not show.** That a range-aware hold must fail: the lock here is a plain count, the evidence hold of follow-ups 12 to 31 is not, and follow-up 13 asks the question of an evidence hold gated by range.

**What it does not say.** That 80 m, a far run of three and a lock of thirty are the right numbers (one point of the plane; the near phantom problem is a property of marking at the first reading and holding for thirty, not of the 80 m). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 11: THE CONFIRMATION RULE ITSELF (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-confirm"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 10 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. Follow-ups 7 and 10 both end by listing "an M-of-N rule" as untried, and "confirm" is the word the detector's marking half has carried since `nav-sensed-confirm-returns`. It is taken here as **the confirmation rule itself**: the detector of follow-ups 7 to 10 marks a cell after `k` occupied readings IN A ROW; this round asks whether `M` occupied readings among the last `N` do better, the track-initiation rule of a radar. If it meant a replication of the detector's result on fresh seeds, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question.** A run of `k` in a row is brittle against the sonar's misses: with a miss chance `m` a real cell is read occupied twice running with chance (1 - m) squared (0.64 at `m` = 0.2, 0.36 at 0.4), and a marked cell that one miss clears has to start its run again (the map that forgets clears on the first free reading). An `M`-of-`N` rule keeps a short memory of the readings, so a cell cleared by a miss is marked again at the next hit if one of the two before it was a hit: a real cell is marked about half the time at `m` = 0.4 against 0.36 for a run of two, and, which is the point, **re-marks at once**. The price is false returns: a false return passes a 2-of-3 rule with chance about three times the rate squared, against once for a run of two, and a 2-of-4 six times.

**The rule.** A free cell is marked when, among its last `N` readings (a reading counted at each ping that ensonifies it), at least `M` were occupied; `N` readings back is the cell's own history, not the last `N` pings. `(M, M)` is a run of `M` and `(1, 1)` is the map that forgets. The clear is a single free reading (`clear` 1), so the confirmation is the only thing that varies.

**Conditions** (follow-up 4's white faults, everything else as follows-ups 3 to 10): **`W`**: misses 0.2, first surfaces only, a white 2 degree heading error and the false rate **0.01** uniform (follow-up 7's `W`); **`V`**: the same with the misses at **0.4** (the sonar misses most of what it should see); and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; the six rules **`m1-1`** (the map that forgets), **`m2-2`** and **`m3-3`** (runs of two and three), **`m2-3`** (the designated), **`m2-4`** and **`m3-4`** under `W` and under `V` (12 arms); and `m2-3@clean`. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD16 = seeds 1601 to 1660 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m2-3` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `W` and under `V`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** Inflation by the plan (18 m round each occupied cell) already covers a hole of one cell in a wall, so a flickering real cell may cost little, and then the `M`-of-`N` rule buys nothing; and a window of four ages the readings of a cell the vessel has been moving past (a reading three pings ago was taken from 9 m back).

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **N1** an `M`-of-`N` rule is kinder to misses than a run: under `V`, `m2-3` reaches at least 5 more than `m2-2`. (A run of two is marked 36% of the time at a miss chance of 0.4, a 2-of-3 rule about half.)
* **N2** and no worse where the misses are mild: under `W`, `m2-3` is within 8 of `m2-2` (either side). (Three times the false-return chance of a run of two is still 0.0003 a cell a ping.)
* **N3** a third reading buys little: under `W`, `m3-3` is within 8 of `m2-2` (either side).
* **N4** every confirmation rule repairs the white false returns: under `W` each of `m2-2`, `m3-3`, `m2-3`, `m2-4` and `m3-4` reaches at least 20 more than `m1-1`.
* **N5** `m2-3` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `W`.
* **N6** and under `V`.
* **N7** on a sea with no faults `m2-3@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **N8** a stricter rule is slower and pays for it where misses are many: under `V`, `m3-4` reaches at least 5 fewer than `m2-3`. (A 3-of-4 rule at a hit chance of 0.6 is met 48% of the time, a 2-of-3 rule 65%.)

**What it would not say.** That 2-of-3 is the best of the family (six rules are a sample; a leaky counter, a median or a log-likelihood sum are untried); that the readings of a cell are independent of one another (the vessel moves between pings: they are not, and the persistent faults of follow-up 5 would break the independence on purpose); that the numbers carry to the range-aware or held detectors of follow-ups 8 to 10 (only the mark is varied, the clear is a single free reading). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.
