### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 11 (2026-11-03; HELD16 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorConfirmHeld16.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: SUPPORTED (6 of 6 cells).** The designated `m2-3` against 80% of exact's count: W: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current HELD; V: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current HELD. **4 of 8 predictions held; N1, N2, N3, N4 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 172 (59/58/55); `long+fix+forget` 171 (59/57/55); `m1-1@W` 139 (39/58/42); `m2-2@W` 166 (56/59/51); `m3-3@W` 148 (51/55/42); `m2-3@W` 157 (53/56/48); `m2-4@W` 163 (55/58/50); `m3-4@W` 156 (53/55/48); `m1-1@V` 125 (35/52/38); `m2-2@V` 148 (55/51/42); `m3-3@V` 106 (35/39/32); `m2-3@V` 152 (50/54/48); `m2-4@V` 152 (51/54/47); `m3-4@V` 123 (39/46/38); `m2-3@clean` 174 (57/60/57).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **N1 MISSED**: an M-of-N rule is kinder to misses than a run: under V, m2-3 reaches at least 5 more than m2-2. m2-3@V 152, m2-2@V 148: +4 against the 5 asked.
* **N2 MISSED**: and no worse where the misses are mild: under W, m2-3 is within 8 of m2-2. m2-3@W 157, m2-2@W 166: 9 apart, within 8 asked.
* **N3 MISSED**: a third reading buys little: under W, m3-3 is within 8 of m2-2. m3-3@W 148, m2-2@W 166: 18 apart, within 8 asked.
* **N4 MISSED**: every confirmation rule repairs the white false returns: under W each of m2-2, m3-3, m2-3, m2-4 and m3-4 reaches at least 20 more than m1-1. m2-2 166, m3-3 148, m2-3 157, m2-4 163, m3-4 156 against m1-1@W 139 (20 asked).
* **N5 HELD**: m2-3 holds the bar in every kind under W. m2-3@W: 53/47.2 56/46.4 48/44.0 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **N6 HELD**: and under V. m2-3@V: 50/47.2 54/46.4 48/44.0 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **N7 HELD**: on a sea with no faults m2-3@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. m2-3@clean 174, long+fix+forget 171: 3 apart, within 8 asked.
* **N8 HELD**: a stricter rule is slower and pays where misses are many: under V, m3-4 reaches at least 5 fewer than m2-3. m2-3@V 152, m3-4@V 123: +29 against the 5 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The designated rule holds both seas, and so do others.** `m2-3` holds all six cells (white false returns at 0.01 and the same with the sonar missing 40%), as `m2-4` does; POST HOC, the six-cell tally of every rule flown is `m2-3` 6, `m2-4` 6, `m2-2` 5 (missing the current kind under V by two flights: 42 of the 44 needed), `m3-4` 3, `m3-3` 2 and `m1-1` (the map that forgets) 2. Four of eight predictions held (N5 to N8).
* **The family collapses at two readings; a third costs, and the memory buys nothing measurable.** Summed over the two seas, `m2-2` reaches 314, `m2-4` 315, `m2-3` 309, `m3-4` 279, `m1-1` 264 and `m3-3` 254 of 360. POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios): `m2-3` against `m2-2` under V +14 / +10 (p 0.54) and under W +4 / +13 (p 0.049: the run of two is the better under mild misses); `m2-3` against `m2-4` +7 / +13 under W (p 0.26) and +13 / +13 under V (p 1.0). N1 (the memory pays under heavy misses) missed by one flight (+4 against 5), N2 (no worse under mild misses) missed by one (9 apart against 8).
* **A third reading is the price.** N3 missed: `m3-3` is 18 below `m2-2` under W and 42 below it under V, and N8 held by a margin: `m3-4` is 29 below `m2-3` under V. The cost shows as collisions, not timeouts: `m3-3@V` collided in 72 of 180 flights with a median minimum clearance of 3.0 m (the other rules 5.8 to 8.7 m, `m3-4` lowest), because a real obstacle is marked late; 8 of its 24 m gaps were reached against 12 for `m2-2`.
* **Every confirmation repairs the white false returns, but by less than the 20 asked for the strictest.** Against `m1-1@W` 139: `m2-2` +27, `m2-4` +24, `m2-3` +18, `m3-4` +17, `m3-3` +9 (N4 missed on `m2-3`, `m3-4` and `m3-3`). POST HOC sign tests: `m2-3@W` +29 / `m1-1@W` +11 (p 0.0064); `m2-3@V` +40 / `m1-1@V` +13 (p 0.0003).
* **Nothing is lost on a sea with no faults.** `m2-3@clean` 174 against the clean map's 171 (N7 held; POST HOC +7 / +4, p 0.55). In this set `exact` reached 172 (7 collided), a harder set than HELD12's 177, and every bar is against the exact count of the set it is flown on.

**What it does not say.** That a window of three or four is not better under some other fault (the faults are two points, and the readings of a cell are not independent: the vessel moves between them); that a rule that is no better here would be no better with a hold or a range (those are follow-ups 12 to 31, flown on their own sets). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 12: THE HOLD RULE ITSELF (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 11 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is the mirror of follow-up 11: there the detector's *marking* rule was the subject, here its *clearing* rule is. Follow-up 7's hold is a count of free readings IN A ROW, the same for every cell, and its registration already names the cost: a hold of twelve on a mark of one makes each false return last twelve pings (G3), and a hold shorter than a dropout does not cover it. The rule tried here is a hold that **scales with the evidence**; a K-of-N free rule and a leaky counter are untried. If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The idea.** A cell read occupied once is weak evidence of an obstacle (it may be a false return); a cell read occupied forty times as the vessel closes on it is strong evidence. Hold them the same and the choice is between keeping every phantom a long time or letting a real obstacle go after twelve pings. **Hold in proportion to the evidence**: a marked cell is cleared when it has read free in a row as many times as it was read occupied since it was marked, up to a cap. A phantom (one or two occupied readings) clears at the first or second free reading; an obstacle the vessel has watched for a minute needs the full cap.

**The rule.** The detector of follow-up 7 with its clear replaced: each cell counts its occupied readings since it was last cleared (`occ`, reset to 0 when it is cleared and when a free reading finds it unmarked); a marked cell is cleared after `min(cap, occ)` free readings in a row (`occ` is at least 1 for a marked cell). The **mark is a single occupied reading** (`mark` 1), so the hold is the only thing that varies. `cap` 1 is the map that forgets; a count of `cap` free readings regardless of `occ` is follow-up 7's hold.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 11): **`D30`** (follow-up 8's: whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law), **`W`** (follow-up 7's: follow-up 4's four white faults at a uniform false rate of 0.01), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`), and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and a mark of 1): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; five holds under each of `D30` and `W` (10 arms): **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`h12`** and **`h30`** (a plain hold of twelve and of thirty free readings in a row), **`e12`** and **`e30`** (the evidence hold with a cap of twelve and of thirty; **`e30`** is the designated); `inc` and `e30` under `P` (2 arms); and `e30@clean`. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD17 = seeds 1701 to 1760 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `e30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `D30`**: three cells, the long dropout the uniform hold of twelve was tuned short of. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W` is in the predictions, not the bar: with a mark of 1 the evidence hold does not repair false returns, only avoids making them worse.)

**The tensions, said before the data.** A cap of thirty is a near-sticky map for any cell the vessel has watched for thirty pings, so the fix smear a long hold brings back (the thick wall that closes a 24 m gap) comes back for the cells that matter most; the evidence hold protects the phantoms, not the walls from the smear. And a cell watched for a long time from far away (a high count at long range) is held as strongly as one watched from near.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **O1** a plain long hold makes false returns far worse: `h30@W` reaches at least 20 fewer than `inc@W`. (Each phantom lasts thirty pings.)
* **O2** the evidence hold does not: `e30@W` is within 10 of `inc@W` (either side). (A phantom has read occupied once or twice, so it is cleared by the first or second free reading, as the incumbent clears it.)
* **O3** the evidence hold covers the long dropout: `e30@D30` reaches at least 10 more than `inc@D30`.
* **O4** at little cost against the plain hold of the same cap: `e30@D30` is within 10 of `h30@D30` (either side).
* **O5** the cap matters: `e12@D30` reaches at least 5 fewer than `e30@D30`. (A cap of twelve ends before a dropout of thirty does.)
* **O6** under the persistent mix, the evidence hold is better than the incumbent: `e30@P` reaches more than `inc@P`.
* **O7** on a sea with no faults `e30@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **O8** a plain hold of twelve already helps with the long dropout: `h12@D30` reaches more than `inc@D30`.

**What it would not say.** That one-for-one is the right ratio between occupied readings and free ones, or that thirty is the cap; that a K-of-N free rule, a leaky counter or a likelihood ratio would not be better; that evidence counted since the last clear carries over a gap (a cell the vessel has swept past and back). Only the clear is varied: the confirmation of follow-ups 7 and 11 and the range-awareness of 8 to 10 are not combined with it. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 12 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD17 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@D30` 15/18; `h12@D30` 14/18; `h30@D30` 15/18; `e12@D30` 16/18; `e30@D30` 17/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `h12@W` 2/18; `h30@W` 3/18; `e12@W` 13/18; `e30@W` 13/18; `inc@P` 16/18; `e30@P` 18/18; `e30@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**What DEV showed that the registration did not say, and is said here so that it is not found afterwards and called a surprise:** `h12@W` reached 2 of 18, `h30@W` reached 3 of 18 (the other arms 13 to 18). It is the mechanism the registrations already name for a near hold with a mark of one or two readings (follow-up 7's `d1-12@W` was 3 of 30 on its DEV): near false returns are marked at once and held for thirty readings, some forty or fifty phantom cells sit within 80 m, the plan has no route and the vessel goes into the true obstacles. It is **not read as a result** (18 scenarios) and it moves **no prediction and no verdict rule**; it means the registered predictions and bar cells that need such an arm to reach 80% of exact are likely to be missed, and the study will say so.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `e30`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD17 = seeds 1701 to 1760 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 12 (2026-11-03; HELD17 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorHoldHeld17.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: SUPPORTED (3 of 3 cells).** The designated `e30` against 80% of exact's count: D30: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current HELD. **6 of 8 predictions held; O5, O6 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 176 (60/60/56); `long+fix+forget` 175 (59/60/56); `inc@D30` 146 (53/43/50); `h12@D30` 153 (59/47/47); `h30@D30` 156 (57/49/50); `e12@D30` 155 (54/50/51); `e30@D30` 157 (56/50/51); `inc@W` 132 (36/60/36); `h12@W` 49 (15/14/20); `h30@W` 53 (18/14/21); `e12@W` 128 (41/60/27); `e30@W` 128 (41/60/27); `inc@P` 157 (57/48/52); `e30@P` 157 (53/58/46); `e30@clean` 174 (60/60/54).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **O1 HELD**: a plain long hold makes false returns far worse: h30@W reaches at least 20 fewer than inc@W. inc@W 132, h30@W 53: +79 against the 20 asked.
* **O2 HELD**: the evidence hold does not: e30@W is within 10 of inc@W. e30@W 128, inc@W 132: 4 apart, within 10 asked.
* **O3 HELD**: the evidence hold covers the long dropout: e30@D30 reaches at least 10 more than inc@D30. e30@D30 157, inc@D30 146: +11 against the 10 asked.
* **O4 HELD**: at little cost against the plain hold of the same cap: e30@D30 is within 10 of h30@D30. e30@D30 157, h30@D30 156: 1 apart, within 10 asked.
* **O5 MISSED**: the cap matters: e12@D30 reaches at least 5 fewer than e30@D30. e30@D30 157, e12@D30 155: +2 against the 5 asked.
* **O6 MISSED**: under the persistent mix, the evidence hold is better than the incumbent: e30@P reaches more than inc@P. e30@P 157 against inc@P 157.
* **O7 HELD**: on a sea with no faults e30@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. e30@clean 174, long+fix+forget 175: 1 apart, within 8 asked.
* **O8 HELD**: a plain hold of twelve already helps with the long dropout: h12@D30 reaches more than inc@D30. h12@D30 153 against inc@D30 146.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The evidence hold does what it was registered to do: a plain hold's dropout cure, without its false-return ruin.** The designated `e30` (a mark of one reading, a clear after as many free readings in a row as it was read occupied, up to 30) holds **all three bar cells** under D30: **SUPPORTED**; six of eight predictions held (O1 to O4, O7, O8; O5 and O6 missed). Under dropouts of 30 pings `e30` reaches 157, the plain hold of thirty 156, the evidence hold of twelve 155, the plain hold of twelve 153 and the map that forgets 146 (O3 held: +11 over `inc` against the 10 asked; POST HOC `e30` against `inc` +20 / +9, p 0.061). Under white false returns `e30` is level with `inc` (128 against 132, O2 held; POST HOC +19 / +23, p 0.64) while the **plain holds are ruinous: `h12@W` 49 and `h30@W` 53** (127 collided each, median minimum clearance -0.01 m, 50% of the grid ensonified; O1 held by 79 flights where 20 were asked; POST HOC `e30` against `h12` +94 / +15 and against `h30` +90 / +15, both p < 0.0001).
* **The cap hardly matters; the evidence does the work.** O5 missed: `e12@D30` 155, `e30@D30` 157 (POST HOC +5 / +3, p 0.73); `e12` and `e30` fly the same flight in all but 2 of 180 scenarios under W and in 90 of 180 under D30. Any of the four holds clears the bar's islands and current cells under D30; the plain hold of twelve misses the gaps (47 against 48.0 needed), as does `inc` (43).
* **The 24 m gaps are where the holds differ.** Under D30, 24 m / 32 m gaps reached: `inc` 8 / 15, `h12` 10 / 17, `h30` 9 / 20, `e12` 13 / 17, `e30` 13 / 17 (exact 20 / 20). The plain hold of thirty fills the map (median 485 occupied cells at the end against `e30`'s 360 and `inc`'s 283) and replans 53 times a flight against 85 to 87: it is cheap in plans and dear when there are false returns.
* **Under the persistent mix and on a sea with no faults it costs nothing.** O6 missed on equality (`e30@P` 157, `inc@P` 157, POST HOC +13 / +13, p 1.0); O7 held: `e30@clean` 174 against the clean map's 175.

**What it does not say.** That one-for-one is the right ratio between occupied and free readings or that the hold is complete: it is shown against a dropout of thirty pings, a made-up fault; follow-ups 13, 15 to 31 put it with a range, a confirmation, a ghost and a confirmed clear. That the evidence hold beats the map that forgets by a margin the sign test can see (p 0.061 under D30, 1.0 under P). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 13: THE HOLD THAT KNOWS BOTH (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-hold"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 12 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is the junction of two registrations: follow-up 8 gives the hold a **range** (a long hold only within `near` of the sonar, one free reading beyond), follow-up 12 gives it **evidence** (a marked cell is cleared after as many free readings in a row as it was read occupied since marking, up to a cap). This round puts them in one hold, and against each alone. If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The idea.** The evidence hold keeps the phantoms light (a cell read once clears at the first free reading) and keeps a watched obstacle (a cell read thirty times needs thirty free readings), but it holds a watched cell the same way at any range, and the hold's one real cost is far from the vessel, where it brings back the fix smear (the thick wall that closes a 24 m gap in the plan at range) and where a dropout is harmless because the next ping re-reads the cell from nearer. So **hold by evidence, but only within `near`**: beyond it, forget at the first free reading.

**The rule** (the detector of follow-up 7 with two clears and an evidence switch): a marked cell **farther than `near`** from the sonar at the ping is cleared after `clear` free readings in a row; one **within `near`** (inclusive; believed position to the cell's centre, at that ping) after `clearNear`; with `evidence` on each threshold is `min(threshold, occ)`, where `occ` counts the cell's occupied readings since it was last cleared (reset to 0 when it is cleared and when a free reading finds it unmarked, at least 1 for a marked cell). The **mark is a single occupied reading** (`mark` 1), so the hold is the only thing that varies.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 12): **`D30`** (follow-up 8's: whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law), **`W`** (follow-up 7's uniform false rate of 0.01 with follow-up 4's other faults), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and a mark of 1): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget` (the map that forgets, no faults); the four holds **`inc`** (1, 1: the map that forgets), **`n30`** (a plain hold of 30 within 80 m, 1 beyond), **`e30`** (the evidence hold, cap 30, at any range) and **`re30`** (**the evidence hold, cap 30, within 80 m, 1 beyond: the designated**) under `D30` (4 arms); `inc`, `e30` and `re30` under `W` (3) and under `P` (3); and `n30`, `e30` and `re30` on a sea with no faults (3). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD18 = seeds 1801 to 1860 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `re30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `D30`**: three cells. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar: the bar there is met by the map that forgets as often as not, and what they ask is what the hold costs.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The range gate is a bet that the fix smear is a far-range cost; the smear is shifted placements of a wall by the fix's errors of 3 m, and a wall that is thickened by 3 m matters most when the vessel is passing it. If it is the near hold that closes the 24 m gap, `re30` and `e30` will cost the same and `n30` too. And a cell the vessel marked from far away, watched for sixty pings and then flown up to, is held as hard as one it saw first from near.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **L1** the range-gated evidence hold covers the long dropout: `re30@D30` reaches at least 10 more than `inc@D30`.
* **L2** and as well as the evidence hold at any range: `re30@D30` is within 10 of `e30@D30` (either side). (A dropout of thirty pings is a near event for the cells that matter.)
* **L3** the range gate is worth something on a clean sea: `re30@clean` reaches more than `e30@clean`. (The far hold brings back the fix smear.)
* **L4** and costs little: `re30@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **L5** it does not make false returns worse: under `W`, `re30` is within 10 of `inc` (either side). (A false return is read once; its cell clears at the first free reading.)
* **L6** a plain near hold does: under `W`, `n30` reaches at least 20 fewer than `inc`. (Every near phantom lasts thirty pings: some eighty on the map at once.)
* **L7** under the persistent mix `P`, `re30` reaches more than `inc`.
* **L8** summed over `D30` and `P`, `re30` reaches more than `e30` and more than `n30`.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, a cap of thirty and one-for-one are the right numbers; that a cell's range at the ping (as against the nearest range it has ever been read from) is the right gate; that the gate should be a step and not a smooth weight. Only the hold is varied: the confirmation of follow-ups 7, 9 and 11 is not combined with it, and a detector with both ends range-aware and evidence-based is a follow-up on this one. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 13 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD18 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**What DEV showed that the registration did not say, and is said here so that it is not found afterwards and called a surprise:** `n30@W` reached 3 of 18 (the other arms 13 to 18). It is the mechanism the registrations already name for a near hold with a mark of one or two readings (follow-up 7's `d1-12@W` was 3 of 30 on its DEV): near false returns are marked at once and held for thirty readings, some forty or fifty phantom cells sit within 80 m, the plan has no route and the vessel goes into the true obstacles. It is **not read as a result** (18 scenarios) and it moves **no prediction and no verdict rule**; it means the registered predictions and bar cells that need such an arm to reach 80% of exact are likely to be missed, and the study will say so.

**A flaw in the registration, found while the specs were written as data and said before any set is flown.** Predictions L6 (*under W, `n30` reaches at least 20 fewer than `inc`*) and L8 (*summed over D30 and P, `re30` reaches more than `e30` and more than `n30`*) name the arms `n30@W` and `n30@P`, which the registered arm list (`inc`, `e30` and `re30` under W and under P) left out; as registered, L6 and L8 could not be scored. **Both arms are ADDED**: the study flies seventeen arms, 3,060 flights, and nothing else about it changes (the designated arm, the bar, the verdict rule and every prediction's wording and threshold stand). The gate's `checkSpec` now refuses a spec whose prediction names an arm the study does not fly.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `re30`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD18 = seeds 1801 to 1860 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 13 (2026-11-03; HELD18 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights (17 arms, 3060 flights), `brain/subSensedDetDetectorRangeHoldHeld18.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: PARTLY SUPPORTED (2 of 3 cells).** The designated `re30` against 80% of exact's count: D30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current HELD. **3 of 8 predictions held; L1, L3, L4, L7, L8 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 177 (60/59/58); `long+fix+forget` 179 (60/60/59); `inc@D30` 155 (55/44/56); `n30@D30` 133 (49/37/47); `e30@D30` 162 (56/53/53); `re30@D30` 153 (53/47/53); `inc@W` 129 (33/59/37); `n30@W` 35 (11/10/14); `e30@W` 130 (35/59/36); `re30@W` 135 (39/59/37); `inc@P` 156 (52/50/54); `n30@P` 126 (46/34/46); `e30@P` 161 (52/54/55); `re30@P` 156 (53/51/52); `n30@clean` 154 (51/53/50); `e30@clean` 173 (58/60/55); `re30@clean` 167 (53/59/55).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **L1 MISSED**: the range-gated evidence hold covers the long dropout: re30@D30 reaches at least 10 more than inc@D30. re30@D30 153, inc@D30 155: -2 against the 10 asked.
* **L2 HELD**: and as well as the evidence hold at any range: re30@D30 is within 10 of e30@D30. re30@D30 153, e30@D30 162: 9 apart, within 10 asked.
* **L3 MISSED**: the range gate is worth something on a clean sea: re30@clean reaches more than e30@clean. re30@clean 167 against e30@clean 173.
* **L4 MISSED**: and costs little: re30@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. re30@clean 167, long+fix+forget 179: 12 apart, within 8 asked.
* **L5 HELD**: it does not make false returns worse: under W, re30 is within 10 of inc. re30@W 135, inc@W 129: 6 apart, within 10 asked.
* **L6 HELD**: a plain near hold does: under W, n30 reaches at least 20 fewer than inc. inc@W 129, n30@W 35: +94 against the 20 asked.
* **L7 MISSED**: under the persistent mix P, re30 reaches more than inc. re30@P 156 against inc@P 156.
* **L8 MISSED**: summed over D30 and P, re30 reaches more than e30 and more than n30. re30@D30+re30@P 309 against e30@D30+e30@P 323 ; re30@D30+re30@P 309 against n30@D30+n30@P 259.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The range gate removes the dropout cure; the evidence hold at every range keeps it.** The designated `re30` (the evidence hold of cap 30 within 80 m, one free reading beyond) holds **2 of the three bar cells** (islands and current under D30; the gaps by ONE flight, 47 against the 47.2 needed). It is level with the map that forgets there (153 against 155; L1 asked +10, missed; POST HOC sign test +5 / +7, p 0.77), while **`e30`, the same hold at every range, is the best arm of the set under D30 (162) and holds all three bar cells** (it was not the designated; POST HOC `re30` against `e30` +7 / +16, p 0.093; L2, within 10, held by one flight). **Three of eight predictions held (L2, L5, L6).** This is follow-up 8's finding with the evidence hold: a dropout that begins far away clears the far cells at once, whatever the hold within 80 m.
* **The evidence hold does not make false returns worse, as registered, and the plain near hold ruins them.** L5 held: under W `re30` 135, `e30` 130 and `inc` 129 (POST HOC `re30` against `inc` +20 / +14, p 0.39); L6 held by a wide margin: `n30@W` reached 35 of 180 (127 collided, median minimum clearance -0.02 m, 53% of the grid ensonified) against `inc`'s 129: 94 fewer where 20 were asked. `n30@P` 126 against `inc@P` 156, `n30@D30` 133 against 155 (POST HOC `re30` against `n30` D30 +26 / +6, p 0.0005; W +109 / +9; P +39 / +9; both p < 0.0001). The plain near hold of thirty is the worst arm of every sea, as in follow-ups 7, 8 and 10.
* **The range gate does not buy back the clean-sea cost; it adds to it.** L3 missed (the gate is worth something on a clean sea: `re30@clean` reaches more than `e30@clean`): `e30` 173, `re30` 167, `n30` 154, the clean map 179. L4 (within 8 of the clean map) missed: `re30` is 12 below; POST HOC `e30@clean` +0 / the clean map +6 (p 0.031), `re30@clean` +0 / +12 (p 0.0005), `n30@clean` +0 / +25 (p < 0.0001). The 24 m gaps on the clean sea: `e30` 20 of 20, `re30` 19, `n30` 13 (clean map 20). Any long hold costs a little on a sea with no faults (the fix smear), and the evidence hold at every range costs least.
* **Under the persistent mix the gate gives nothing either.** L7 missed on equality (`re30@P` 156, `inc@P` 156, +12 / +12, p 1.0) and L8 (`re30` above `e30` and `n30` summed over D30 and P) missed: 309 against `e30`'s 323 and `n30`'s 259.
* **The registration's arm list was short, and was mended before the set was flown** (Amendment 1): L6 and L8 named `n30@W` and `n30@P`; the study flew seventeen arms, 3,060 flights.

**What it does not say.** That a range gate cannot pay: this is the gate by the range at the ping, a step at 80 m; follow-up 15 asks which range and which shape, and follow-ups 17 to 19, 24, 25, 27 and 29 put it with a range-aware mark and a confirmed clear. That the evidence hold at every range is a good detector in general: it holds the bar here under dropouts and loses to the map that forgets nowhere in this set, but the clean-sea cost is not nothing. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 14: THE CONFIRMATION THAT KNOWS HOW FAR, WITH A MEMORY (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-confirm"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 13 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is the junction of the two registrations on the marking side: follow-up 9 gives the confirmation a **range** (a run of three readings beyond `near`, one reading within), follow-up 11 gives it a **memory** (`M` occupied readings among the last `N`, not `k` in a row). This round puts the memory into the range-aware confirmation. If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The idea.** Follow-up 9's far confirmation is a run of three, and where the range law holds the misses grow with the range as well as the false returns (0.4 at 200 m): a run of three is read about a fifth of the time for a real obstacle at the sonar's edge (0.6 cubed), so the far end of the long sonar is thinned, the very range it was bought for. A rule with a memory (2 of the last 3) is met about two thirds of the time at the same miss chance and passes a false return with chance three times the rate squared, which at the far end's 0.06 is 0.011 a cell a ping against 0.0036... but the point is the real obstacle: **seen early, marked early, re-marked at once after a miss**. So: **near, one reading; far, `M` of `N`**.

**The rule** (the detector of follow-up 11 with a second rule for cells within `near`): a free cell **farther than `near`** from the sonar at the ping (inclusive; believed position to the cell's centre) is marked when at least `M` of its last `N` readings were occupied; a cell **within `near`** when at least `Mn` of its last `Nn` were (here 1 of 1). The clear is a single free reading (`clear` 1), so the confirmation is the only thing that varies.

**Conditions** (follow-up 4's four white faults, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 13): **`R3`** and **`R1`** (follow-up 9's: the misses and the false returns following the range law, the false rate **0.03** or **0.01** at its mean); **`W`** (follow-up 7's: the false rate uniform at 0.01, the law NOT holding); and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and a clear of 1): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3` the seven rules **`inc`** (1-of-1: the map that forgets), **`c2`** (2 in a row everywhere), **`m23`** (2-of-3 everywhere: follow-up 11's designated), **`cr`** (3 in a row beyond 80 m, 1 within: follow-up 9's designated), **`rm23`** (**2-of-3 beyond 80 m, 1 within: the designated**), **`rm24`** (2-of-4 beyond, 1 within) and **`rm34`** (3-of-4 beyond, 1 within) (7 arms); `inc`, `m23` and `rm23` under `W` (3); `m23` and `rm23` under `R1` (2); and `rm23@clean`. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD19 = seeds 1901 to 1960 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm23` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3` and under `R1`**: six cells, the two conditions where the range law holds. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W` is in the predictions, not the bar: it asks what the bet on the law costs.)

**The tension, said before the data.** A window of three ages the readings of a cell the vessel has been moving past: a reading two pings back was taken from 6 m farther away, and the far readings it keeps are the noisiest. And a near zone marked at the first reading has, under the range law at `R3`, some one and a third phantom cells a ping inside 80 m against about a fiftieth for a 2-of-3 rule there: the bet is the same as follow-up 9's.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **S1** a memory pays far away: under `R3`, `rm23` reaches at least 5 more than `cr`. (A run of three far away is read 22% of the time for a real obstacle at 200 m, a 2-of-3 rule 65%.)
* **S2** and is no worse than the same rule everywhere: under `R3`, `rm23` is within 10 of `m23` (either side). (No delay near, 60 times the near phantoms.)
* **S3** a longer window buys nothing: under `R3`, `rm24` is within 8 of `rm23` (either side).
* **S4** a stricter rule pays where the misses are many: under `R3`, `rm34` reaches at least 5 fewer than `rm23`.
* **S5** the bet on the law has a price: under `W`, `rm23` reaches at least 10 fewer than `m23`.
* **S6** `rm23` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3`.
* **S7** and under `R1`.
* **S8** on a sea with no faults `rm23@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 2-of-3 and 1-of-1 are the right numbers; that the window should be a step in range and not a smooth function; that the readings of a cell are independent (the vessel moves between them); that the range law is true of a real sonar. Only the confirmation is varied: the hold of follow-ups 7, 8, 12 and 13 is not combined with it, and a detector with both ends range-aware and with a memory at each is the table this and follow-up 13 point at. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 14 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD19 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@R3` 16/18; `c2@R3` 18/18; `m23@R3` 18/18; `cr@R3` 14/18; `rm23@R3` 16/18; `rm24@R3` 16/18; `rm34@R3` 17/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `m23@W` 17/18; `rm23@W` 11/18; `m23@R1` 17/18; `rm23@R1` 15/18; `rm23@clean` 16/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `rm23`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD19 = seeds 1901 to 1960 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 14 (2026-11-03; HELD19 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorRangeConfirmHeld19.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: PARTLY SUPPORTED (5 of 6 cells).** The designated `rm23` against 80% of exact's count: R3: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current MISSED; R1: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current HELD. **7 of 8 predictions held; S6 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 176 (58/60/58); `long+fix+forget` 172 (59/58/55); `inc@R3` 149 (46/60/43); `c2@R3` 155 (53/56/46); `m23@R3` 159 (55/58/46); `cr@R3` 131 (50/37/44); `rm23@R3` 154 (52/58/44); `rm24@R3` 149 (49/56/44); `rm34@R3` 139 (54/38/47); `inc@W` 130 (42/58/30); `m23@W` 156 (51/59/46); `rm23@W` 109 (38/44/27); `m23@R1` 158 (53/60/45); `rm23@R1` 158 (54/56/48); `rm23@clean` 173 (58/60/55).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **S1 HELD**: a memory pays far away: under R3, rm23 reaches at least 5 more than cr. rm23@R3 154, cr@R3 131: +23 against the 5 asked.
* **S2 HELD**: and is no worse than the same rule everywhere: under R3, rm23 is within 10 of m23. rm23@R3 154, m23@R3 159: 5 apart, within 10 asked.
* **S3 HELD**: a longer window buys nothing: under R3, rm24 is within 8 of rm23. rm24@R3 149, rm23@R3 154: 5 apart, within 8 asked.
* **S4 HELD**: a stricter rule pays where the misses are many: under R3, rm34 reaches at least 5 fewer than rm23. rm23@R3 154, rm34@R3 139: +15 against the 5 asked.
* **S5 HELD**: the bet on the law has a price: under W, rm23 reaches at least 10 fewer than m23. m23@W 156, rm23@W 109: +47 against the 10 asked.
* **S6 MISSED**: rm23 holds the bar in every kind under R3. rm23@R3: 52/46.4 58/48.0 44/46.4 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **S7 HELD**: and under R1. rm23@R1: 54/46.4 56/48.0 48/46.4 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **S8 HELD**: on a sea with no faults rm23@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. rm23@clean 173, long+fix+forget 172: 1 apart, within 8 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The memory rescues the range-aware idea from follow-up 9's collapse, and brings it level with the uniform rule, not above it.** The designated `rm23` (2-of-3 beyond 80 m, one reading within, a clear of one) holds **5 of the six bar cells**: all three under R1, islands and gaps under R3, and misses the current kind under R3 by two flights (44 against the 46.4 needed). **Seven of eight predictions held (S1 to S5, S7, S8); S6 missed.** Against follow-up 9's `cr` it is 23 flights higher under R3 (154 against 131; POST HOC sign test `rm23` +33 / `cr` +10, p 0.0006).
* **But it is no better than the same rule at every range where the law holds, and far worse where it does not.** S2 held (`rm23@R3` 154, `m23@R3` 159, 5 apart); POST HOC sign tests: `rm23` against `m23` under R3 +10 / +15 (p 0.42) and under R1 +11 / +11 (p 1.0); under the uniform false rate `rm23@W` is **47 below** `m23@W` (109 against 156; S5 asked 10, held; POST HOC +8 / +55, p < 0.0001), with 61 of 180 collided, a median minimum clearance of 4.3 m and 13 of 20 of the 24 m gaps. The bet on the law has the registered price and the range-aware mark pays no dividend where the law holds.
* **The uniform 2-of-3 is the better detector on the count, and it was not the designated.** POST HOC, the six-cell tally over R3 and R1: `rm23` 5, `m23` 4 (it misses the current kind by one flight under both seas: 46 and 45 against 46.4 needed), `c2` 2 and `inc` 1 (the last two flown under R3 only). `m23` against `inc` under W is follow-up 7's cure again (156 against 130).
* **The third reading is the price again.** Both rules with a third hit far away collapse in the narrow gaps: 24 m / 32 m gaps reached under R3, `rm23` 20 / 18, `rm24` 16 / 20, **`rm34` 6 / 12, `cr` 7 / 12**. S4 held (`rm34` 15 below `rm23`; POST HOC +26 / +11 for `rm23`, p 0.020) and S3 held (`rm24` 5 below `rm23`, +13 / +8, p 0.38). This is follow-up 11's finding (a third reading costs; the memory does not repair it) seen at 80 m.
* **Nothing is lost on a sea with no faults.** `rm23@clean` 173 against the clean map's 172 (S8 held; POST HOC +4 / +3, p 1.0).

**What it does not say.** That a range-aware mark cannot pay: this is the near rule of one reading, a clear of one and the step at 80 m; follow-ups 17, 18, 19, 25 and 26 put it with a hold, and follow-up 28 gives the near zone a light confirmation. That the uniform 2-of-3 is the best mark (follow-up 11: 2-of-2, 2-of-3 and 2-of-4 are one family). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 15: WHICH RANGE THE HOLD GOES BY (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-range"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 14 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name, and it is follow-up 13's name (`detector-range-hold`) with the two words the other way round. Taken as the same two ideas with the emphasis moved: follow-up 13 gave the hold a range gate and **assumed** the range (the cell's range from the vessel *at the ping that reads it free*, a step at 80 m) and its shape; both choices are named there as untried (*"a cell's range at the ping (as against the nearest range it has ever been read from)"*, *"a step and not a smooth weight"*). **This round makes the hold's range the subject: which range does the gate go by, and is it a step or a ramp.** If it meant follow-up 13 itself, `re30` is in this study as an arm and the round can be read as that with its controls; if it meant a sweep of the hold's length (a range of caps), follow-up 12 has caps of 12 and 30 and the ramp here is a continuum from 1 to 30. Say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question.** A marked cell's hold depends on how far the cell is from the vessel, but a cell has several distances: the one **now** (at this ping), the one it was **first marked** from, and the **nearest** it has ever been read occupied from. A wall first seen at 150 m and approached to 30 m is far at marking, near now and nearest-ever at 30 m; a wall the vessel has passed is near at its nearest and far now. Which of the three says "this cell is one a dropout would be dangerous for" is not obvious, and the answer sets what the hold keeps: *now* keeps what the vessel is close to, *marking* keeps what it was close to when it first saw it (which is nothing, for the long sonar's whole point is to see early), and *nearest* keeps what it has ever come close to (including a wall already behind it).

**The rule** (follow-up 13's evidence hold with the gate's range and shape chosen): a marked cell is cleared after `thr` free readings in a row, `thr = min(occ, g(r))` where `occ` is the count of its occupied readings since it was last cleared (reset to 0 when it is cleared and when a free reading finds it unmarked; at least 1 for a marked cell), and `g(r)` is the gate, a function of a range `r`:
* **step**: `g(r) = 30` if `r <= 80 m`, else 1;
* **ramp**: `g(r) = max(1, round(30 x (1 - r / 200 m)))`, so 30 at the vessel, 18 at 80 m, 12 at 120 m, 1 at the sonar's edge.

The **range `r`** is: **`ping`** (the believed position to the cell's centre, at the ping that reads it free); **`mark`** (the same, at the ping that marked it, kept); **`min`** (the least such range over the pings that read it occupied since it was last cleared). The **mark is a single occupied reading** (`mark` 1), so the hold is the only thing that varies.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 14, and exactly follow-up 13's four): **`D30`** (whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law), **`W`** (the uniform false rate of 0.01 with follow-up 4's other faults), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and a mark of 1): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `D30` the six holds **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`re30`** (step, `ping`: follow-up 13's designated, here a control), **`mk30`** (step, `mark`), **`mn30`** (step, `min`), **`rp30`** (ramp, `ping`) and **`rn30`** (**ramp, `min`: the designated**); `inc`, `re30` and `rn30` under `W` (3); `inc` and `rn30` under `P` (2); `re30` and `rn30` on a sea with no faults (2). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD20 = seeds 2001 to 2060 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rn30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `D30`**: three cells, as follow-up 13's. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar, for follow-up 13's reason: the bar there is met by the map that forgets as often as not, and what they ask is what the hold costs.)

**Why `rn30` is the designated, and the tension, said before the data.** The ramp on the nearest range ever read is the hold that says *a cell I have ever come close to I trust, in proportion to how close*; it is the one choice that does not depend on where the vessel happens to be at the instant of the dropout. Against it: the ramp is **shorter than the step inside 80 m** (20 at 70 m, 18 at 80 m where the step holds 30) and **longer outside it** (12 at 120 m where the step holds 1), so it gives up near hold for the 80 to 160 m band, where follow-up 13 bet the fix smear costs (the thick wall that closes a 24 m gap in a plan at range); and `min` keeps a wall held after the vessel has passed it, which is harmless if a plan from the vessel to the goal seldom uses the cells behind, and costs if it does (a sub in a current that is swept back).

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **T1** the range at marking is the wrong range: `mk30@D30` reaches at least 10 fewer than `re30@D30`. (A cell marked from 150 m is a far cell for ever, and the vessel that has closed on it to 30 m is back at a hold of one just when a dropout begins.)
* **T2** the nearest range ever read does as well as the range now: `mn30@D30` is within 10 of `re30@D30` (either side). (A vessel closing on a wall: the nearest range ever read *is* the range now until it passes.)
* **T3** the ramp does as well as the step: `rp30@D30` is within 10 of `re30@D30` (either side). (They differ only between 40 and 160 m.)
* **T4** the designated covers the long dropout: `rn30@D30` reaches at least 10 more than `inc@D30`.
* **T5** `rn30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `D30`.
* **T6** and costs little on a sea with no faults: `rn30@clean` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **T7** and does not make false returns worse: under `W`, `rn30` is within 10 of `inc` (either side). (A false return is read once, so `occ` is 1 and its cell clears at the first free reading whatever the gate says.)
* **T8** under the persistent mix `P`, `rn30` reaches more than `inc`.
`re30@clean`, `re30@W` and the per-kind and per-gap counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 200 m, 30 and one-for-one are the right numbers; that a step and a linear ramp are the only shapes (an exponential, or a ramp on the *square* of the range, which is the law the faults follow, are untried); that the nearest range ever read since the last clear is the right memory (the range at which it was last read, a decayed minimum, the mean, are untried); that the same gate suits the confirmation (that is follow-ups 9, 10 and 14, with a different rule at each end). Only the hold is varied, and only its gate: the confirmation of follow-ups 7, 9 and 11 is not combined with it. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 15 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD20 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@D30` 15/18; `re30@D30` 17/18; `mk30@D30` 15/18; `mn30@D30` 17/18; `rp30@D30` 16/18; `rn30@D30` 16/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `re30@W` 14/18; `rn30@W` 14/18; `inc@P` 16/18; `rn30@P` 18/18; `re30@clean` 18/18; `rn30@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `rn30`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD20 = seeds 2001 to 2060 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 15 (2026-11-03; HELD20 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorHoldRangeHeld20.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: SUPPORTED (3 of 3 cells).** The designated `rn30` against 80% of exact's count: D30: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current HELD. **4 of 8 predictions held; T1, T3, T4, T8 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 176 (59/60/57); `long+fix+forget` 173 (59/55/59); `inc@D30` 160 (60/43/57); `re30@D30` 153 (57/41/55); `mk30@D30` 153 (57/41/55); `mn30@D30` 155 (58/42/55); `rp30@D30` 167 (59/52/56); `rn30@D30` 164 (58/49/57); `inc@W` 128 (39/59/30); `re30@W` 132 (40/58/34); `rn30@W` 121 (39/55/27); `inc@P` 157 (56/51/50); `rn30@P` 156 (52/52/52); `re30@clean` 173 (59/60/54); `rn30@clean` 176 (60/59/57).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **T1 MISSED**: the range at marking is the wrong range: mk30@D30 reaches at least 10 fewer than re30@D30. re30@D30 153, mk30@D30 153: +0 against the 10 asked.
* **T2 HELD**: the nearest range ever read does as well as the range now: mn30@D30 is within 10 of re30@D30. mn30@D30 155, re30@D30 153: 2 apart, within 10 asked.
* **T3 MISSED**: the ramp does as well as the step: rp30@D30 is within 10 of re30@D30. rp30@D30 167, re30@D30 153: 14 apart, within 10 asked.
* **T4 MISSED**: the designated covers the long dropout: rn30@D30 reaches at least 10 more than inc@D30. rn30@D30 164, inc@D30 160: +4 against the 10 asked.
* **T5 HELD**: rn30 holds the bar in every kind under D30. rn30@D30: 58/47.2 49/48.0 57/45.6 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **T6 HELD**: and costs little on a sea with no faults: rn30@clean is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. rn30@clean 176, long+fix+forget 173: 3 apart, within 8 asked.
* **T7 HELD**: and does not make false returns worse: under W, rn30 is within 10 of inc. rn30@W 121, inc@W 128: 7 apart, within 10 asked.
* **T8 MISSED**: under the persistent mix P, rn30 reaches more than inc. rn30@P 156 against inc@P 157.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The designated hold holds the bar, and what carries it is the ramp, not the range.** `rn30` (the evidence hold of cap 30, a ramp to 200 m, gated by the nearest range the cell has been read occupied from) holds **all three bar cells** under D30 (islands 58, gaps 49, current 57 against 47.2, 48.0 and 45.6 needed): **SUPPORTED**. Four of eight predictions held (T2, T5, T6, T7). It does so because the **ramp** reaches the 24 m gaps that every STEP gate leaves: gaps reached under D30, `inc` 43, `re30` 41, `mk30` 41, `mn30` 42 (all below the 48.0 needed), **`rp30` 52, `rn30` 49**; the 24 m gaps alone, `inc` 8, `re30` 8, `mk30` 7, `mn30` 8, `rp30` 14, `rn30` 11 of 20 (exact 20, clean map 15).
* **Which range the gate goes by does not matter; its shape does.** T1 (the range at marking is the wrong range, +10 for the range now) missed: `re30` (ping) and `mk30` (marking) both reach 153, `mn30` (nearest read) 155; T2 held. T3 (the ramp does as well as the step) missed **the other way round**: `rp30` is 14 above `re30` (167 against 153), and the three step arms are all *below* the map that forgets (160) under D30. POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios): `rn30` against `re30` +18 / +7 (p 0.043), against `mk30` +17 / +6 (p 0.035), against `mn30` +16 / +7 (p 0.093), against `rp30` +1 / +4 (p 0.38: the nearest range ever read adds nothing to the ramp). A likely reason, consistent with the registered tension and not isolated by an arm: the ramp holds 12 readings at 120 m and 6 at 160 m where the step holds one beyond 80 m, so a dropout that begins far away does not clear the far cells at once.
* **It is not clearly better than the map that forgets.** T4 (+10 over `inc` under D30) missed: `rn30` 164, `inc` 160 (POST HOC +12 / +8, p 0.50), and the bar's margin over `inc` is the gaps kind alone (49 against 43). Under the persistent mix `rn30` 156 and `inc` 157 (T8, "more than", missed; +12 / +13, p 1.0); under the uniform false rate `rn30` 121, `re30` 132 and `inc` 128 (T7 held, within 10; the ramp is the lowest of the three, POST HOC `rn30` against `re30` +15 / +26, p 0.12).
* **Nothing is lost on a sea with no faults.** T6 held: `rn30@clean` 176 against the clean map's 173 (POST HOC +6 / +3, p 0.51); `re30@clean` 173.

**What it does not say.** That the ramp beats the step in general (one ramp, to 200 m, against one step, at 80 m, under one dropout), that the range does not matter (the three ranges differ only in what a dropout of 30 pings does to a cell the vessel is closing on, and the step arms are all poor for another reason), or that the bar is a margin over the map that forgets (it is not: only the gaps kind separates them). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 16: THE CONFIRMATION AND THE HOLD, BOTH BETTER (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-confirm"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 15 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. Follow-up 7 put a confirmation and a hold into one detector, but both in their plainest form (`k` occupied readings in a row to mark, `kc` free readings in a row to clear). Follow-up 11 then improved the *confirmation* alone (an `M`-of-`N` rule, with a clear of one free reading) and follow-up 12 the *hold* alone (in proportion to the evidence, with a mark of one occupied reading); follow-ups 13 and 14 did the same pairing for the range-aware versions. **This round is the pairing for the two rules themselves**: the `M`-of-`N` mark of follow-up 11 and the evidence hold of follow-up 12 in one detector, against each alone and against follow-up 7's plain pair. If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown. One other reading is left out on purpose: *confirming the clear*, a K-of-N rule on the free readings (follow-up 12 names it untried); it is a different rule at the other end, not a pairing of these two.

**The idea.** The two improvements answer different faults: the memory in the mark answers white false returns and a high miss rate (it re-marks at once after a miss and asks for a repeat before marking at all), the evidence in the hold answers dropouts without making phantoms last. Neither is expected to do the other's work (the mark alone loses an obstacle to a dropout, the hold alone, with a mark of one, takes every false return), so the joint condition, with both faults at once, is where a pairing could earn its place.

**The rule** (follow-ups 11 and 12 in one detector). Each cell keeps a window of its last `N` readings (a reading is counted at each ping that ensonifies it) and `occ`, the number of occupied readings since it was last cleared. A free cell is **marked** when at least `M` of its window were occupied (`occ` then starts at the number of occupied readings in the window); a marked cell is **cleared** after `min(cap, occ)` consecutive free readings (`occ` rises by one at each occupied reading while marked; at clearing, `occ` and the window are emptied). `(M, N) = (1, 1)` with `cap` 1 is the map that forgets. A **plain hold** (`h`) clears after `cap` consecutive free readings whatever `occ` is.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 15):
* **`W`**: follow-up 4's four white faults (misses 0.2, first surfaces only, a white 2 degree heading error) with the false rate **0.01** uniform (follow-up 7's `W`).
* **`D30`**: whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law (follow-up 8's), and no other fault.
* **`WD30`**: both at once, `W`'s four faults and false rate and `D30`'s dropouts: **the stress the pairing is made for**.
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `WD30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`d2-12`** (follow-up 7's designated: a run of 2 to mark, a plain hold of 12), **`m23`** (2-of-3 to mark, one free reading to clear), **`e30`** (one occupied reading to mark, the evidence hold with a cap of 30), **`m23h30`** (2-of-3 to mark, a plain hold of 30) and **`m23e30`** (**2-of-3 to mark, the evidence hold with a cap of 30: the designated**); `inc`, `m23` and `m23e30` under `W` (3); `inc`, `e30` and `m23e30` under `D30` (3); and `m23e30` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD21 = seeds 2101 to 2160 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m23e30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `WD30`**: three cells, the joint stress. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `D30` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar: they ask whether each fault is still cured and what the pair costs when there is nothing to cure.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The two rules interact through the **marking window**. The hold protects only a cell that has been marked, and the confirmation makes the unmarked stretch longer: a dropout of 30 pings that begins before an obstacle has been confirmed hides it from the confirmation as well as from the hold, and nothing is held. On the long sonar the first hits on a wall come at 150 to 200 m, where the dropout rate is highest (the range law), so a pair that asks for two hits before it holds anything may protect less than the hold alone would have. Against that, the evidence hold's one job was keeping phantoms light, and a confirmation has already removed nearly all of them: the evidence hold may have little left to do after it (the prediction **U3** is that).

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **U1** the pair does what neither half does: under `WD30`, `m23e30` reaches more than `m23` and more than `e30`. (`m23` lets a dropout take the obstacle; `e30` takes every false return, as `inc` does under `W`.)
* **U2** and does better than follow-up 7's plain pair: under `WD30`, `m23e30` reaches at least 10 more than `d2-12`. (A plain hold of twelve ends before a dropout of thirty does.)
* **U3** after a confirmation the evidence hold has little left to protect: under `WD30`, `m23h30` is within 10 of `m23e30` (either side). (A phantom that passes a 2-of-3 rule is a rare event, 0.0003 a cell a ping at a false rate of 0.01, so a plain hold of thirty keeps few.)
* **U4** the pair still covers the long dropout: under `D30`, `m23e30` reaches at least 10 more than `inc`.
* **U5** and the pair still repairs the white false returns: under `W`, `m23e30` reaches at least 20 more than `inc`. (Follow-up 7's G1 and follow-up 11's N4, with the evidence hold added.)
* **U6** `m23e30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `WD30`.
* **U7** on a sea with no faults `m23e30` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **U8** the mark's delay costs the pair nothing on dropouts: under `D30`, `m23e30` is within 10 of `e30` (either side). (The tension above, scored: if it holds, the hiding of an unconfirmed obstacle by a dropout is not the cost it could be.)
`d2-12` against `inc` under `WD30`, the per-kind and per-gap counts, and the cells' `occ` at the moment of marking are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 2-of-3, 30 and one-for-one are the right numbers (one point of the plane, as follow-ups 11 and 12's); that the pair is better than any other detector (a K-of-N clear, a median, a likelihood sum, the filtered fix, are untried); that the readings of a cell are independent (the vessel moves between them; the persistent faults of follow-up 5 break that on purpose, and are not in this study); that `occ` counted since the last clear carries over a sweep past and back. Neither the range-awareness of follow-ups 8 to 10, 13 to 15 nor the persistent mix is combined with the pair: the table of those is the next one. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 16 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD21 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@WD30` 8/18; `d2-12@WD30` 14/18; `m23@WD30` 14/18; `e30@WD30` 13/18; `m23h30@WD30` 8/18; `m23e30@WD30` 12/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `m23@W` 17/18; `m23e30@W` 18/18; `inc@D30` 15/18; `e30@D30` 17/18; `m23e30@D30` 17/18; `m23e30@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

*Reported and not predicted* in the registration was *the cells' `occ` at the moment of marking*: it is not kept per cell in the flight record and is **not reported**; the record carries `detStats` (marks, clears) and the pooled occupied cells at the last plan.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `m23e30`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD21 = seeds 2101 to 2160 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 16 (2026-11-03; HELD21 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorHoldConfirmHeld21.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: PARTLY SUPPORTED (2 of 3 cells).** The designated `m23e30` against 80% of exact's count: WD30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current HELD. **4 of 8 predictions held; U1, U2, U3, U6 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 173 (60/59/54); `long+fix+forget` 171 (58/57/56); `inc@WD30` 101 (38/34/29); `d2-12@WD30` 129 (47/39/43); `m23@WD30` 135 (48/41/46); `e30@WD30` 103 (35/39/29); `m23h30@WD30` 105 (35/41/29); `m23e30@WD30` 130 (52/29/49); `inc@W` 124 (40/55/29); `m23@W` 162 (51/59/52); `m23e30@W` 162 (53/57/52); `inc@D30` 147 (58/43/46); `e30@D30` 161 (57/49/55); `m23e30@D30` 160 (57/50/53); `m23e30@clean` 175 (58/59/58).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **U1 MISSED**: the pair does what neither half does: under WD30, m23e30 reaches more than m23 and more than e30. m23e30@WD30 130 against m23@WD30 135 ; m23e30@WD30 130 against e30@WD30 103.
* **U2 MISSED**: and does better than follow-up 7's plain pair: under WD30, m23e30 reaches at least 10 more than d2-12. m23e30@WD30 130, d2-12@WD30 129: +1 against the 10 asked.
* **U3 MISSED**: after a confirmation the evidence hold has little left to protect: under WD30, m23h30 is within 10 of m23e30. m23h30@WD30 105, m23e30@WD30 130: 25 apart, within 10 asked.
* **U4 HELD**: the pair still covers the long dropout: under D30, m23e30 reaches at least 10 more than inc. m23e30@D30 160, inc@D30 147: +13 against the 10 asked.
* **U5 HELD**: and the pair still repairs the white false returns: under W, m23e30 reaches at least 20 more than inc. m23e30@W 162, inc@W 124: +38 against the 20 asked.
* **U6 MISSED**: m23e30 holds the bar in every kind under WD30. m23e30@WD30: 52/48.0 29/47.2 49/43.2 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **U7 HELD**: on a sea with no faults m23e30 is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. m23e30@clean 175, long+fix+forget 171: 4 apart, within 8 asked.
* **U8 HELD**: the mark's delay costs the pair nothing on dropouts: under D30, m23e30 is within 10 of e30. m23e30@D30 160, e30@D30 161: 1 apart, within 10 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The pair has both cures when the faults come one at a time and does not add them when they come together.** The designated `m23e30` (a 2-of-3 mark, the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range) holds **2 of the three bar cells** under WD30 (islands 52, current 49; the gaps kind 29 against the 47.2 needed): **PARTLY SUPPORTED**; four of eight predictions held (U4, U5, U7, U8). Alone, each fault is cured as registered: under D30 `m23e30` reaches 160 against `inc`'s 147 (U4 held, +13) and level with the evidence hold alone (161; U8 held, POST HOC +3 / +4, p 1.0); under W it reaches 162 against `inc`'s 124 (U5 held, +38) and level with the 2-of-3 mark alone (162; +11 / +11, p 1.0).
* **Under both at once nothing is cured much, and the pair is not the best arm.** WD30 counts of 180: `m23` **135**, `m23e30` 130, `d2-12` 129, `m23h30` 105, `e30` 103, `inc` 101 (exact 173). U1 missed (the pair beats each half: it beats `e30` by 27 and loses to `m23` by 5), U2 missed (+1 over follow-up 7's plain pair where 10 were asked), U6 missed on the gaps kind. POST HOC sign tests (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios): `m23e30` against `m23` +24 / +29 (p 0.58), against `d2-12` +29 / +28 (p 1.0), against `e30` +47 / +20 (p 0.0013), against `m23h30` +48 / +23 (p 0.0041), against `inc` +47 / +18 (p 0.0004). The mark does the work under false returns and the hold adds nothing measurable on top of it when both faults are present.
* **The narrow gaps are where the pair loses.** WD30, 24 m / 32 m gaps reached: `m23` 8 / 15, `d2-12` 10 / 12, `e30` 8 / 13, `m23h30` 10 / 16, **`m23e30` 3 / 8**, `inc` 6 / 9 (exact 19 / 20): the evidence hold behind a confirmed mark holds the fix-smeared walls that close the gaps, with no false returns for the hold to be cheap against. Not isolated by an arm: the gaps are the registered tension of follow-up 16 (*a dropout that begins before an obstacle is confirmed hides it from the hold*) and the smear of follow-up 21 as well.
* **A plain hold is worse than the evidence hold after a confirmation too.** U3 missed by 15: `m23h30@WD30` 105, `m23e30@WD30` 130 (13 left the arena and 16 timed out for the plain hold, against 2 and 0). The registered reason (a phantom that passes 2-of-3 is rare, so there is little to protect) is not what the data show; the cause is not isolated (`m23h30` was not flown under W alone).
* **Nothing is lost on a sea with no faults.** U7 held: `m23e30@clean` 175 against the clean map's 171 (POST HOC +6 / +2, p 0.29).

**What it does not say.** That the evidence hold is useless behind a confirmation: it is shown level with the mark alone under both single faults and under the pair of faults, in a set where the gaps kind decides the bar; follow-ups 17 to 31 put range, a ghost and a confirmed clear with it. That WD30 (white false returns of 0.01 with leak-free dropouts of 30 pings) is a sample of faults. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 17: THE WHOLE DETECTOR, EVERY END KNOWING HOW FAR (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-confirm-range"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 16 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 16's name with `-range` on the end, and follow-ups 13 and 14 each end by pointing at this table: *"a detector with both ends range-aware and evidence-based is a follow-up on this one"* (13), *"a detector with both ends range-aware and with a memory at each is the table this and follow-up 13 point at"* (14). So it is taken as **the pairing of follow-up 16 with range**: the memory mark of follow-up 14 (one reading within `near`, 2-of-3 beyond) and the evidence hold of follow-up 13 (cap 30 within `near`, one free reading beyond) in one detector, against follow-up 16's uniform pair, follow-up 10's range-aware detector built on the plain rules, and each range-aware half alone. If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown. Both ends use the range at the ping and a step at 80 m, the choices follow-ups 10, 13 and 14 made and follow-up 15 questions; if follow-up 15 finds a better gate it is a swap for a later round.

**The idea, in one line.** Near the vessel, where a reading is reliable and a mistake is deadly, **mark at the first reading and hold what has been watched**; far from it, where a reading is unreliable and a mistake is cheap, **ask for a repeat with a memory and forget at the first free reading**.

**The rule** (follow-up 16's detector with a second pair of thresholds for the cells within `near`): each cell keeps a window of its last `N` readings and `occ`, the occupied readings since it was last cleared. A free cell **farther than `near`** from the sonar at the ping (believed position to the cell's centre) is marked when at least `M` of its window were occupied, one **within `near`** (inclusive) when at least `Mn` were; a marked cell **farther than `near`** is cleared after `min(clear, occ)` consecutive free readings, one **within `near`** after `min(clearNear, occ)`. The designated: `M`-of-`N` = 2-of-3 and `Mn` = 1-of-1 beyond and within 80 m, `clear` = 1 and `clearNear` = 30 (follow-up 14's mark and follow-up 13's hold). With the near values equal to the far ones it is follow-up 16's detector.

**Conditions** (everything else as follows-ups 3 to 16):
* **`R3D30`**: follow-up 4's four white faults with the misses and the false returns following the range law (`R3`: the false rate **0.03** at its mean, 0.06 at 200 m, 0.0054 at 60 m) and follow-up 8's whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law: **the stress the range-aware pair is made for**, and the bar's.
* **`W`**: the same four faults with the false rate **uniform** at 0.01 and no dropouts (follow-up 7's), the law NOT holding.
* **`P`**: follow-up 5's `pall`, the persistent mix (a bias, dropouts of ten pings, persistent false returns, the range law, first surfaces only).
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23e30`** (follow-up 16's designated, the same rules at every range), **`rd`** (follow-up 10's designated: mark 3 beyond and 1 within, a plain hold of 30 within and 1 beyond), **`rm23`** (follow-up 14's designated alone: the range-aware mark, a clear of 1), **`re30`** (follow-up 13's designated alone: the range-aware evidence hold, a mark of 1) and **`rm23re30`** (**the designated: both**); `inc`, `m23e30` and `rm23re30` under `W` (3) and under `P` (3); and `rm23re30` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD22 = seeds 2201 to 2260 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm23re30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30`**: three cells, the joint stress where the law holds. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar: they ask what the bet on the law, the persistent faults and nothing to cure cost.)

**The tension, said before the data.** Every earlier tension holds at once, and one is new. **A dropout that begins far away takes the cell at once**: a cell marked from 190 m is held for one free reading beyond 80 m, so a dropout that starts at 150 m clears it, and the near hold of thirty has nothing left to hold when the vessel arrives; the cell comes back only when the dropout ends, 30 pings (90 m of travel) later, at 60 m. The near hold protects the cells that were *already near* when the dropout began, which the range law makes the rarer ones (a dropout's rate falls with the square of the range). That is follow-up 15's question (which range the gate goes by) in its sharpest form, and a range gate on the range at the ping, as here, is the choice that cannot answer it. Against that the two range bets compound: a first-reading mark inside 80 m, where under `R3` the false returns are rare and a miss is deadly, is the same bet as follow-ups 9, 10 and 14, and `W` is where it is called.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **Q1** range-awareness pays where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30` reaches at least 10 more than `m23e30`. (No delay and a long hold near, a memory and a short hold far.)
* **Q2** the rules of 11 and 12 pay in their range-aware form: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30` reaches at least 5 more than `rd`. (A memory far away against a run of three; evidence near against a count.)
* **Q3** neither half does the other's work: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30` reaches more than `rm23` and more than `re30`.
* **Q4** `rm23re30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **Q5** the bet on the law has a price: under `W`, `rm23re30` reaches at least 10 fewer than `m23e30`. (Follow-up 10's M4 and follow-up 14's S5 asked of the pair: the near zone marks a false return at the first reading.)
* **Q6** but it is still a cure: under `W`, `rm23re30` reaches at least 10 more than `inc`. (The far 84% of the sector's area is confirmed.)
