* **Q7** under the persistent mix `P`, `rm23re30` is within 10 of `m23e30` (either side). (Persistent false returns repeat, so a confirmation passes them in either, and the hold is the same for the cells that matter.)
* **Q8** on a sea with no faults `rm23re30` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`rm23re30` against `inc` under `R3D30` and `P`, `rd` and `m23e30` against each other, the per-kind and per-gap counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 2-of-3, 1-of-1, 30 and one-for-one are the right numbers (one point of a large plane); that the range at the ping is the right gate (follow-up 15); that a step in range and not a smooth function is right; that the range law is true of a real sonar; that the detector is better than any other (a K-of-N clear, a median, a likelihood sum and the filtered fix are untried). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 17 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD22 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@R3D30` 14/18; `m23e30@R3D30` 15/18; `rd@R3D30` 1/18; `rm23@R3D30` 12/18; `re30@R3D30` 16/18; `rm23re30@R3D30` 13/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `m23e30@W` 18/18; `rm23re30@W` 12/18; `inc@P` 16/18; `m23e30@P` 17/18; `rm23re30@P` 13/18; `rm23re30@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**What DEV showed that the registration did not say, and is said here so that it is not found afterwards and called a surprise:** `rd@R3D30` reached 1 of 18 (the other arms 12 to 18). It is the mechanism the registrations already name for a near hold with a mark of one or two readings (follow-up 7's `d1-12@W` was 3 of 30 on its DEV): near false returns are marked at once and held for thirty readings, some forty or fifty phantom cells sit within 80 m, the plan has no route and the vessel goes into the true obstacles. It is **not read as a result** (18 scenarios) and it moves **no prediction and no verdict rule**; it means the registered predictions and bar cells that need such an arm to reach 80% of exact are likely to be missed, and the study will say so.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `rm23re30`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD22 = seeds 2201 to 2260 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 17 (2026-11-03; HELD22 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorHoldConfirmRangeHeld22.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (0 of 3 cells).** The designated `rm23re30` against 80% of exact's count: R3D30: islands MISSED, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **3 of 8 predictions held; Q1, Q3, Q4, Q6, Q7 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 175 (60/59/56); `long+fix+forget` 172 (57/59/56); `inc@R3D30` 125 (45/47/33); `m23e30@R3D30` 145 (54/40/51); `rd@R3D30` 14 (8/2/4); `rm23@R3D30` 119 (49/29/41); `re30@R3D30` 112 (42/37/33); `rm23re30@R3D30` 118 (45/29/44); `inc@W` 128 (43/57/28); `m23e30@W` 163 (56/56/51); `rm23re30@W` 97 (37/33/27); `inc@P` 149 (51/48/50); `m23e30@P` 161 (57/53/51); `rm23re30@P` 142 (55/39/48); `rm23re30@clean` 166 (57/59/50).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **Q1 MISSED**: range-awareness pays where the law holds: under R3D30, rm23re30 reaches at least 10 more than m23e30. rm23re30@R3D30 118, m23e30@R3D30 145: -27 against the 10 asked.
* **Q2 HELD**: the rules of 11 and 12 pay in their range-aware form: under R3D30, rm23re30 reaches at least 5 more than rd. rm23re30@R3D30 118, rd@R3D30 14: +104 against the 5 asked.
* **Q3 MISSED**: neither half does the other's work: under R3D30, rm23re30 reaches more than rm23 and more than re30. rm23re30@R3D30 118 against rm23@R3D30 119 ; rm23re30@R3D30 118 against re30@R3D30 112.
* **Q4 MISSED**: rm23re30 holds the bar in every kind under R3D30. rm23re30@R3D30: 45/48.0 29/47.2 44/44.8 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **Q5 HELD**: the bet on the law has a price: under W, rm23re30 reaches at least 10 fewer than m23e30. m23e30@W 163, rm23re30@W 97: +66 against the 10 asked.
* **Q6 MISSED**: but it is still a cure: under W, rm23re30 reaches at least 10 more than inc. rm23re30@W 97, inc@W 128: -31 against the 10 asked.
* **Q7 MISSED**: under the persistent mix P, rm23re30 is within 10 of m23e30. rm23re30@P 142, m23e30@P 161: 19 apart, within 10 asked.
* **Q8 HELD**: on a sea with no faults rm23re30 is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. rm23re30@clean 166, long+fix+forget 172: 6 apart, within 8 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The range-aware pair misses the bar in all three kinds, and the uniform pair does better than it everywhere.** The designated `rm23re30` (a 2-of-3 mark beyond 80 m and a first-reading mark within, an evidence hold of cap 30 within 80 m and one free reading beyond) holds **0 of the three bar cells** under R3D30: islands 45 against the 48.0 needed, gaps 29 against 47.2, current 44 against 44.8: **NOT SUPPORTED**; three of eight predictions held (Q2, Q5, Q8). R3D30 counts of 180: `m23e30` (follow-up 16's pair, the same rules at every range) **145**, `inc` 125, `rm23` 119, `rm23re30` 118, `re30` 112, `rd` 14 (exact 175, clean `long+fix+forget` 172). Q1 missed by a wide margin: `rm23re30` reaches 27 **fewer** than `m23e30` where 10 **more** were asked; POST HOC (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios, no correction for the number of tests) `rm23re30` against `m23e30` +12 / +39, p 0.0002.
* **The law did not buy the range-aware design anything even where it holds.** Q3 missed: `rm23re30` 118 against `rm23` 119 and `re30` 112 (it beats one half by 6 and loses to the other by 1; POST HOC against `rm23` +17 / +18, p 1.0, against `re30` +36 / +30, p 0.54, against `inc` +31 / +38, p 0.47). Q2 held (+104 over `rd`) but says more about `rd` than about `rm23re30`: `rd` (follow-up 10's designated, a mark of one within 80 m with a plain hold of 30) reached 14 of 180 with 85 collisions, 44 timeouts and 37 flights that left the arena. That collapse is the one Amendment 1 recorded from the DEV set before this set was flown (near false returns marked at once and held thirty readings), not a surprise found afterwards.
* **Under the uniform false rate the near zone does harm and the range-aware pair is worse than doing nothing.** Q5 held (`rm23re30@W` 97 against `m23e30@W` 163: 66 fewer, 10 fewer asked), Q6 missed (97 against `inc@W` 128: 31 **fewer** where 10 **more** were asked; POST HOC +18 / +49, p 0.0002; 70 collisions of 180 against 13 for `m23e30` and 42 for `inc`). Mean marks per flight under W were `rm23re30` 702, `m23e30` 222, `inc` 2,024 (median plans 132.5, 90 and 134). That is in the direction of the registered tension (the first-reading mark within 80 m takes the false returns the confirmation would have filtered) but no arm isolates the near zone under W: `rm23` and `re30` were flown under R3D30 only.
* **The persistent mix and a sea with no faults.** Q7 missed: under P `rm23re30` 142 against `m23e30` 161, 19 apart where 10 were allowed (POST HOC +6 / +25, p 0.0009; `inc@P` 149). Q8 held: `rm23re30@clean` 166 against the clean map's 172 (6 apart; POST HOC +4 / +10, p 0.18; islands 57 against 57, gaps 59 against 59, current 50 against 56).
* **Where the cells fall.** POST HOC the arm that would have held the most bar cells under R3D30 is `m23e30` (islands 54, gaps 40, current 51: two of three, PARTLY SUPPORTED by the same rule); `rm23` held one (islands), `inc`, `re30`, `rm23re30` and `rd` none. The gaps kind is where `rm23re30` is worst (24 m / 32 m / 40 m gaps reached 2 / 9 / 18 of 20, against `m23e30` 9 / 11 / 20 and `inc` 10 / 19 / 18); the map that forgets does best on the gaps (47 of 60) and worst on the current kind (33 of 60) under R3D30, the reverse of the pairs.

**What it does not say.** That a range-aware detector cannot win: 80 m, 2-of-3, one reading within, a cap of 30 and one-for-one are one point of a large plane, and the part that failed here (a mark of one within 80 m, shared by `rd`, `rm23` and `rm23re30`) is a part some of follow-ups 18 to 31 vary. That the range law is the wrong model of a real sonar (it is the generator's, and the uniform pair did better under it). That the near zone is the cause under W (no arm isolates it). The late-mark count the registration named was not computed (Amendment 1). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 18: THE SECOND HOLD, ON WHAT WAS CONFIRMED (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-confirm-range-hold"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 17 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 17's name (`hold-confirm-range`) with a second `-hold` on the end, and follow-up 17's registration ends on the problem a second hold could answer: **a range-gated hold lets a dropout that begins far away take the cell at once**, and a confirmed cell cleared by one miss far away needs its confirmation again (a 2-of-3, with the window emptied at the clear). Taken here as **a second hold, on what has been confirmed**, in the two ways it can be done and in all four combinations:
* **hold the cell**: take the range gate off the hold (the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range, the mark still range-aware): `rm23e30`;
* **hold the confirmation (the ghost)**: a marked cell cleared after at least 4 occupied readings is not forgotten for 60 readings of that cell, and in that time a single occupied reading re-marks it (at any range, whatever the mark rule). The plan sees a ghost as free; only its re-marking is quickened.
If it meant only one of these, or something else (a hold on the unmarked candidate is the `M`-of-`N` window already; a hold on the hold, a clear that itself needs confirming, is the K-of-N clear follow-ups 12 and 16 name untried), say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The rule** (follow-up 17's detector with two switches). Each cell keeps a window of its last `N` readings, `occ` (occupied readings since last cleared) and a ghost counter. Mark: at least `M` of the window beyond `near`, `Mn` within (designated 2-of-3 and 1-of-1, `near` 80 m). Clear: after `min(clear, occ)` consecutive free readings beyond `near`, `min(clearNear, occ)` within; **the hold at every range** is `clear` = `clearNear` = 30. **The ghost**: when a marked cell is cleared with `occ >= 4`, it becomes a ghost with a life of 60; at each reading of a ghost cell its life falls by one (at zero the window and `occ` are emptied: forgotten); a ghost cell read occupied is marked at that reading, whatever the rule, with `occ` restored to 4. With no ghost and `clear` 1 beyond `near`, it is follow-up 17's detector.

**Conditions** (exactly follow-up 17's, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 17): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 4's four white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law: the bar's), **`W`** (the uniform false rate of 0.01, the law NOT holding), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23e30`** (follow-up 16's uniform pair), **`rm23re30`** (follow-up 17's designated: the range-aware mark and hold), **`rm23e30`** (the hold at every range), **`rm23re30g`** (follow-up 17's detector with the ghost) and **`rm23e30g`** (**the designated: the hold at every range and the ghost**), which is the 2 x 2 of {range-gated hold, hold at every range} x {no ghost, ghost} with the uniform pair and the incumbent beside it; `inc`, `rm23re30` and `rm23e30g` under `W` (3) and under `P` (3); `rm23e30g` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD23 = seeds 2301 to 2360 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm23e30g` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30`**: three cells, follow-up 17's bar on a fresh set. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The hold at every range is the hold follow-up 13 bet *against* (it brings back the fix smear far from the vessel, which thickens a wall and closes a 24 m gap in the plan at range), so this round takes the bet the other way and sees what it costs. And the ghost is a re-marking at one reading, which is what a persistent false return that once passed the confirmation will take advantage of: under `P`, whose false returns repeat by design, a ghost brings a phantom back at its first reading each time it is cleared, and the ghost's job (restoring a real wall at one reading) is the same act.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **J1** the range gate on the hold costs under the joint stress: under `R3D30`, `rm23e30` reaches at least 5 more than `rm23re30`. (Follow-up 17's tension: a dropout that begins far away takes the cell, and a hold of one beyond 80 m lets it.)
* **J2** and the hold at every range costs nothing where the law fails: under `W`, `rm23e30g` is within 10 of `rm23re30` (either side). (A false return is read once or twice, so its `occ` is small and its hold short wherever it is.)
* **J3** the ghost helps when the hold is range-gated: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30g` reaches at least 5 more than `rm23re30`. (A cell cleared far away by a miss or a dropout comes back at one reading and not at two of three.)
* **J4** and little when the hold is everywhere: under `R3D30`, `rm23e30g` is within 8 of `rm23e30` (either side). (A cell held at every range is not cleared by a miss; there is nothing to re-mark.)
* **J5** `rm23e30g` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **J6** the pair of holds beats the uniform pair: under `R3D30`, `rm23e30g` reaches at least 10 more than `m23e30`.
* **J7** under the persistent mix `P`, `rm23e30g` reaches more than `inc`.
* **J8** on a sea with no faults `rm23e30g` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`rm23e30g` against `rm23re30` under `P` and `R3D30`, the per-kind and per-gap counts, and the number of re-marks by ghosts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers for the ghost, or that it should be a count of readings and not a length of time; that a ghost re-marked at one reading is safe against persistent phantoms (`P` is where it is called); that the hold at every range and the ghost are the only second holds (a decayed `occ`, a hold on the nearest range read — follow-up 15 — and a K-of-N clear are untried); that 80 m, 2-of-3 and 30 are the right numbers. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 18 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD23 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@R3D30` 14/18; `m23e30@R3D30` 15/18; `rm23re30@R3D30` 13/18; `rm23e30@R3D30` 14/18; `rm23re30g@R3D30` 14/18; `rm23e30g@R3D30` 16/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `rm23re30@W` 12/18; `rm23e30g@W` 13/18; `inc@P` 16/18; `rm23re30@P` 13/18; `rm23e30g@P` 18/18; `rm23e30g@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

The ghost is as follow-up 23's amendment describes it (window and evidence kept while the ghost lives; the ordinary mark rule can re-mark a ghost cell; with no neighbour test the ghost re-marks on one reading). *Reported and not predicted*: the number of re-marks by ghosts is in the record (`detStats.ghostMarks`).

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `rm23e30g`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD23 = seeds 2301 to 2360 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 18 (2026-11-03; HELD23 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorHoldConfirmRangeHoldHeld23.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (1 of 3 cells).** The designated `rm23e30g` against 80% of exact's count: R3D30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **4 of 8 predictions held; J2, J5, J6, J7 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 176 (59/60/57); `long+fix+forget` 173 (59/58/56); `inc@R3D30` 126 (44/40/42); `m23e30@R3D30` 135 (51/35/49); `rm23re30@R3D30` 124 (47/29/48); `rm23e30@R3D30` 134 (55/33/46); `rm23re30g@R3D30` 133 (51/31/51); `rm23e30g@R3D30` 139 (57/38/44); `inc@W` 125 (36/58/31); `rm23re30@W` 108 (38/40/30); `rm23e30g@W` 121 (33/54/34); `inc@P` 160 (57/49/54); `rm23re30@P` 142 (53/39/50); `rm23e30g@P` 159 (54/53/52); `rm23e30g@clean` 174 (59/60/55).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **J1 HELD**: the range gate on the hold costs under the joint stress: under R3D30, rm23e30 reaches at least 5 more than rm23re30. rm23e30@R3D30 134, rm23re30@R3D30 124: +10 against the 5 asked.
* **J2 MISSED**: and the hold at every range costs nothing where the law fails: under W, rm23e30g is within 10 of rm23re30. rm23e30g@W 121, rm23re30@W 108: 13 apart, within 10 asked.
* **J3 HELD**: the ghost helps when the hold is range-gated: under R3D30, rm23re30g reaches at least 5 more than rm23re30. rm23re30g@R3D30 133, rm23re30@R3D30 124: +9 against the 5 asked.
* **J4 HELD**: and little when the hold is everywhere: under R3D30, rm23e30g is within 8 of rm23e30. rm23e30g@R3D30 139, rm23e30@R3D30 134: 5 apart, within 8 asked.
* **J5 MISSED**: rm23e30g holds the bar in every kind under R3D30. rm23e30g@R3D30: 57/47.2 38/48.0 44/45.6 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **J6 MISSED**: the pair of holds beats the uniform pair: under R3D30, rm23e30g reaches at least 10 more than m23e30. rm23e30g@R3D30 139, m23e30@R3D30 135: +4 against the 10 asked.
* **J7 MISSED**: under the persistent mix P, rm23e30g reaches more than inc. rm23e30g@P 159 against inc@P 160.
* **J8 HELD**: on a sea with no faults rm23e30g is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. rm23e30g@clean 174, long+fix+forget 173: 1 apart, within 8 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The designated holds one bar cell of three, and it has the highest count of the six detectors.** The designated `rm23e30g` (a 2-of-3 mark beyond 80 m and a first-reading mark within, the evidence hold of cap 30 **at every range**, and the ghost) holds the islands cell under R3D30 (57 against 47.2) and misses the gaps (38 against 48.0) and the current kind (44 against 45.6, short by 1.6 flights): **NOT SUPPORTED**; four of eight predictions held (J1, J3, J4, J8). R3D30 counts of 180: `rm23e30g` **139**, `m23e30` 135, `rm23e30` 134, `rm23re30g` 133, `inc` 126, `rm23re30` 124 (exact 176, clean `long+fix+forget` 173). POST HOC (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios, no correction for the number of tests) the top four are level: `rm23e30g` against `m23e30` +24 / +20 (p 0.65), against `rm23e30` +11 / +6 (p 0.33), against `rm23re30g` +23 / +17 (p 0.43); against `inc` +32 / +19 (p 0.09) and against `rm23re30` +28 / +13 (p 0.0275). POST HOC the bar tally: `m23e30`, `rm23e30` and `rm23re30g` each hold islands and current (two of three) and `rm23e30g` holds islands only; the designated misses a cell its siblings hold by 1.6 flights while having the highest total, which is where the cells happen to fall; no arm here isolates the ghost's effect on one kind.
* **Taking the range gate off the hold pays, and the ghost adds a little on either hold.** J1 held: under R3D30 `rm23e30` 134 against `rm23re30` 124 (+10, where 5 were asked), so follow-up 13's bet against the hold at every range (that it thickens far walls) does not show in these totals. J3 held: the ghost on the gated hold, `rm23re30g` 133 against `rm23re30` 124 (+9, 5 asked). J4 held: the ghost on the hold at every range, `rm23e30g` 139 against `rm23e30` 134 (+5, within the 8 allowed). The two ghost effects point the same way and neither was tested as a pair; the mean ghost re-marks per flight were 13 (`rm23re30g`) and 10 (`rm23e30g`) under R3D30, **25 on a sea with no faults**, so the re-marks are not all of the kind the ghost was built for.
* **The pair of holds does not beat the uniform pair.** J6 missed: `rm23e30g` 139 against `m23e30` 135 (+4 where 10 were asked). The first-reading mark within 80 m, the only difference between them besides the ghost, is not shown to cost or pay here. Descriptively, across the two sets that have flown `rm23re30` (follow-ups 17 and 18, different scenarios): R3D30 118 and 124, W 97 and 108, P 142 and 142, against `m23e30` 145 and 135 on R3D30. The size of the gap between the range-aware and the uniform pair moves with the set (-27, then -11).
* **Under the uniform false rate the hold at every range does the better of the two, and J2 missed in that direction.** J2 asked for within 10 either side: `rm23e30g@W` 121 against `rm23re30@W` 108, **13 above**. POST HOC against `rm23re30` +41 / +28 (p 0.15) and against `inc@W` 125, level (+29 / +33, p 0.70). The 24 m gaps under W: `rm23e30g` 16 of 20, `rm23re30` 9, `inc` 19; collisions 44, 49 and 47 of 180, and 9 (`rm23e30g`) and 11 (`rm23re30`) flights that left the arena. The registered reason (a false return is read once or twice, so its evidence is small) is consistent with `rm23e30g` being level with `inc`; the range-gated pair's loss is not isolated by an arm (`rm23e30` and `rm23re30g` were flown under R3D30 only).
* **Under the persistent mix and a sea with no faults.** J7 missed by one flight: under P `rm23e30g` 159 against `inc` 160 ("more than" asked; POST HOC +16 / +17, p 1.0), while `rm23re30@P` is 142 (`rm23e30g` against it +28 / +11, p 0.0095). The ghost's registered price under P (a persistent phantom that earned its evidence is re-marked at one reading) is not visible in the totals, with 16 re-marks a flight. J8 held: `rm23e30g@clean` 174 against 173 (POST HOC +4 / +3, p 1.0; all three kinds hold the bar).
* **The gaps kind is the limit again.** Under R3D30 no detector held the gaps cell (best, `inc`, 40; `rm23e30g` 38; `m23e30` 35; `rm23re30` 29 against the 48.0 needed), as in follow-ups 16, 17 and 20; the 24 m gaps reached of 20 were `rm23e30g` 5, `inc` 9, `m23e30` 3, `rm23re30` 2.

**What it does not say.** That the ghost is worth its re-marks (the effects are +9 and +5 of 180, untested as pairs, in a set where the top four are level), that 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers, or that the hold at every range is right in general (follow-up 13 bet against it and this set did not show the cost; the 24 m gaps are where it would). That the range-aware mark is worse or better than the uniform one (the gap between the two moves with the set). The late-mark count and the re-marks that were of the persistent kind were not computed. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 19: THE MEMORY CONFIRMATION WITH THE PLAIN LOCK (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-confirm-hold"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 18 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 14's name (`detector-range-confirm`, the range-aware confirmation with a memory) with `-hold` on the end, and follow-up 18 did the same for follow-up 17. Follow-up 14 stops at the mark (a clear of one free reading); follow-up 17 adds the *evidence* hold. Taken here as **the same range-aware, 2-of-3 confirmation with the other hold already in the notes, the plain range-gated lock of follow-up 8** (thirty free readings within 80 m, one beyond), which fills the one cell of the table the registered rounds leave empty:

| mark \ hold | none (clear 1) | plain lock (30 near, 1 far) | evidence hold (30 near, 1 far) |
|---|---|---|---|
| run of 3 far, 1 near | follow-up 9 (`cr`) | follow-up 10 (`rd`) | *not registered* (flown here, `crre30`) |
| 2-of-3 far, 1 near | follow-up 14 (`rm23`) | **this round (`rm23lk`)** | follow-up 17 (`rm23re30`) |

So the round asks two things the other two cannot: **what the memory is worth in follow-up 10's range-aware detector**, and **whether the evidence hold's extra machinery earns its place against the plain lock once a confirmation has done its work** (follow-up 16's U3, asked of the range-aware pair). If it meant something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The rule** (follow-up 17's detector without the hold at every range and without the ghost). Each cell keeps a window of its last `N` readings and `occ`. Mark: at least `M` of the window beyond `near`, `Mn` within (designated 2-of-3 and 1-of-1, `near` 80 m). Clear: after `clear` consecutive free readings beyond `near`, `clearNear` within; **the plain lock** `lk` is `clear` 1, `clearNear` 30; **the evidence hold** is `min(30, occ)` within `near` and 1 beyond (follow-up 13's). `rd` is follow-up 10's: a run of 3 beyond and 1 within to mark, the plain lock to clear.

**Conditions** (exactly follow-ups 17 and 18's, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 18): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 4's four white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law: the bar's), **`W`** (the uniform false rate of 0.01, the law NOT holding), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`rm23`** (follow-up 14's designated, no hold), **`rd`** (follow-up 10's designated: a run of 3 far, the plain lock), **`rm23lk`** (**the designated: 2-of-3 far, the plain lock**), **`rm23re30`** (follow-up 17's designated: 2-of-3 far, the evidence hold) and **`crre30`** (a run of 3 far, the evidence hold); `inc`, `rm23lk` and `rm23re30` under `W` (3) and under `P` (3); `rm23lk` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD24 = seeds 2401 to 2460 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm23lk` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30`**: three cells, as follow-ups 17 and 18's. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The plain lock is the hold the evidence hold was made to improve on: **it keeps every near phantom thirty readings**. Under `W` (the law NOT holding: 0.01 a cell a ping everywhere) a near cell is marked at its first false return and held thirty pings, some forty phantoms inside 80 m at once, against the evidence hold's one reading per phantom. The round's bet is the opposite of follow-up 16's U3: that the plain lock is as good as the evidence hold *where the law holds* (R3D30) and **worse where it does not** (`W`), which is the price of simplicity and the reason the evidence hold exists.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **B1** the memory pays in follow-up 10's detector: under `R3D30`, `rm23lk` reaches at least 5 more than `rd`. (A 2-of-3 rule against a run of 3 beyond 80 m, the lock the same.)
* **B2** the evidence hold does not beat the plain lock where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30` is within 10 of `rm23lk` (either side). (Follow-up 16's U3: a confirmation removes nearly all the phantoms the evidence hold exists to keep light.)
* **B3** a lock on top of the range-aware memory pays against dropouts: under `R3D30`, `rm23lk` reaches at least 10 more than `rm23`.
* **B4** `rm23lk` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **B5** where the law does not hold the plain lock is the worse hold: under `W`, `rm23lk` reaches at least 10 fewer than `rm23re30`. (Near phantoms are marked at their first reading and held thirty.)
* **B6** under the persistent mix `P`, `rm23lk` reaches more than `inc`.
* **B7** on a sea with no faults `rm23lk` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
* **B8** the kind of hold matters little with a run of 3 to mark: under `R3D30`, `crre30` is within 10 of `rd` (either side).
`rm23lk` against `inc` under `R3D30` and `W`, `rm23re30` against `inc`, the per-kind and per-gap counts, and the near-phantom counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 2-of-3, 1-of-1 and 30 are the right numbers; that the table's two empty cells are the whole of what is missing (a hold of 12, an evidence hold of another cap, a smooth range gate, follow-up 15's other ranges, follow-up 18's ghost); that the range at the ping is the right gate. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 19 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD24 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@R3D30` 14/18; `rm23@R3D30` 12/18; `rd@R3D30` 1/18; `rm23lk@R3D30` 2/18; `rm23re30@R3D30` 13/18; `crre30@R3D30` 9/18; `inc@W` 14/18; `rm23lk@W` 4/18; `rm23re30@W` 12/18; `inc@P` 16/18; `rm23lk@P` 10/18; `rm23re30@P` 13/18; `rm23lk@clean` 17/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**What DEV showed that the registration did not say, and is said here so that it is not found afterwards and called a surprise:** `rd@R3D30` reached 1 of 18, `rm23lk@R3D30` reached 2 of 18, `rm23lk@W` reached 4 of 18 (the other arms 9 to 18). It is the mechanism the registrations already name for a near hold with a mark of one or two readings (follow-up 7's `d1-12@W` was 3 of 30 on its DEV): near false returns are marked at once and held for thirty readings, some forty or fifty phantom cells sit within 80 m, the plan has no route and the vessel goes into the true obstacles. It is **not read as a result** (18 scenarios) and it moves **no prediction and no verdict rule**; it means the registered predictions and bar cells that need such an arm to reach 80% of exact are likely to be missed, and the study will say so.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `rm23lk`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD24 = seeds 2401 to 2460 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 19 (2026-11-03; HELD24 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorRangeConfirmHoldHeld24.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (0 of 3 cells).** The designated `rm23lk` against 80% of exact's count: R3D30: islands MISSED, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **1 of 8 predictions held; B1, B2, B3, B4, B6, B7, B8 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 175 (58/59/58); `long+fix+forget` 171 (54/59/58); `inc@R3D30` 127 (42/47/38); `rm23@R3D30` 135 (49/34/52); `rd@R3D30` 11 (5/1/5); `rm23lk@R3D30` 10 (5/1/4); `rm23re30@R3D30` 131 (48/32/51); `crre30@R3D30` 118 (50/20/48); `inc@W` 118 (32/56/30); `rm23lk@W` 38 (19/10/9); `rm23re30@W` 109 (32/46/31); `inc@P` 151 (54/45/52); `rm23lk@P` 113 (43/23/47); `rm23re30@P` 140 (50/40/50); `rm23lk@clean` 161 (51/55/55).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **B1 MISSED**: the memory pays in follow-up 10's detector: under R3D30, rm23lk reaches at least 5 more than rd. rm23lk@R3D30 10, rd@R3D30 11: -1 against the 5 asked.
* **B2 MISSED**: the evidence hold does not beat the plain lock where the law holds: under R3D30, rm23re30 is within 10 of rm23lk. rm23re30@R3D30 131, rm23lk@R3D30 10: 121 apart, within 10 asked.
* **B3 MISSED**: a lock on top of the range-aware memory pays against dropouts: under R3D30, rm23lk reaches at least 10 more than rm23. rm23lk@R3D30 10, rm23@R3D30 135: -125 against the 10 asked.
* **B4 MISSED**: rm23lk holds the bar in every kind under R3D30. rm23lk@R3D30: 5/46.4 1/47.2 4/46.4 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **B5 HELD**: where the law does not hold the plain lock is the worse hold: under W, rm23lk reaches at least 10 fewer than rm23re30. rm23re30@W 109, rm23lk@W 38: +71 against the 10 asked.
* **B6 MISSED**: under the persistent mix P, rm23lk reaches more than inc. rm23lk@P 113 against inc@P 151.
* **B7 MISSED**: on a sea with no faults rm23lk is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. rm23lk@clean 161, long+fix+forget 171: 10 apart, within 8 asked.
* **B8 MISSED**: the kind of hold matters little with a run of 3 to mark: under R3D30, crre30 is within 10 of rd. crre30@R3D30 118, rd@R3D30 11: 107 apart, within 10 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The plain lock collapses, with the memory in front of it as without it, and the registered bet that it would hold where the law holds was wrong by a wide margin.** The designated `rm23lk` (2 of 3 beyond 80 m, a first reading within, a plain lock of thirty readings within 80 m and one free reading beyond) reached **10 of 180** under range-law false returns with dropouts (islands 5, gaps 1, current 4 against 46.4, 47.2 and 46.4 needed): **NOT SUPPORTED**, 0 of 3 cells; **one of eight predictions held (B5)**. Follow-up 10's `rd` (a run of three in its place) reached **11**. R3D30 counts of 180: `rm23` (the mark alone) 135, `rm23re30` (the evidence hold in place of the lock) 131, `inc` 127, `crre30` (the run of three with the evidence hold) 118, `rd` 11, `rm23lk` 10 (exact 175, clean `long+fix+forget` 171). B1 missed (`rm23lk` 1 below `rd` where 5 above was asked: the memory is worth nothing behind a plain lock), B2 missed (`rm23re30` is **121 above** `rm23lk` where within 10 was asked), B3 missed (`rm23lk` 125 below `rm23`), B8 missed (`crre30` 107 above `rd`).
* **The failure is in the flights, in the direction the registered tension named.** `rm23lk` under R3D30: 78 collisions, 51 timeouts and 41 flights that left the arena of 180, a median minimum clearance of 2.5 m, and a median of 195 occupied cells at the last plan against 72 for `rm23`, 81 for `rm23re30` and 134 for `inc`. That is consistent with the mechanism follow-up 10's note gives (a first reading within 80 m marks a near false return at once and the lock holds it thirty readings, so the map fills with phantoms and the planner cannot route) and is not isolated by an arm: no arm here has the lock without the first-reading near mark.
* **Where the law fails and under the persistent mix it is worse still, and a clean sea is not free.** B5 held: under W `rm23lk` 38 against `rm23re30` 109 (71 fewer, 10 asked; 124 collisions of 180, median clearance 0, 263 occupied cells at the end). B6 missed: under P `rm23lk` 113 against `inc` 151 (and `rm23re30` 140). B7 missed: `rm23lk@clean` 161 against the clean map's 171, 10 below where within 8 was asked (POST HOC +3 / +13, p 0.021), the same kind of cost follow-up 10's `rd@clean` showed (152 against 170). POST HOC (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios) `rm23lk` against `inc`, `rm23`, `rm23re30` and `crre30` under R3D30 is +1 / +118, +3 / +128, +1 / +122 and +2 / +110, each p below 0.0001, and against `rd` +9 / +10 (p 1.0).
* **The evidence hold is the repair.** The same range-aware marks with the evidence hold in place of the lock are 131 (`rm23re30`) and 118 (`crre30`), against 10 and 11. `crre30` is below `rm23re30` by 13 and gaps are its weak kind (20 of 60 against 32; the 24 m and 32 m gaps reached 1 and 5 of 20); POST HOC. The cell `crre30` fills (a run of three with the evidence hold, R3D30) is the detector follow-up 33 registers for a fresh set; this set already shows its R3D30 count. Follow-up 36, registered expecting the plain lock to be poor whatever its gate, flies the same step lock (`rm23lk`) again with ramps beside it; the registrations of follow-ups 33 and 36 were written before this set was read and are not changed by it.

**What it does not say.** That a lock cannot work: the lock is a plain count of thirty with a first-reading near mark, follow-ups 12 to 17 replace the count with evidence, and the arm that would isolate the near mark (a lock behind a 2-of-3 mark at every range) was not flown. That the range law is true of a real sonar (under it the near zone is the cheap one and the lock collapsed anyway). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 20: WHICH CONFIRMATION WHEN THE HOLD IS THERE (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-confirm-hold"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 19 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 16's name (`detector-hold-confirm`) with the two words the other way round, the same move follow-up 15 made on follow-up 13. Follow-up 16 paired the two improved rules and asked **whether the pair beats each alone** (the hold the subject, the confirmation one of its two halves); this round asks the question the other way: **with the hold in place, which confirmation rule is the right one, and does the choice still matter?** Follow-up 11's family (`M`-of-`N`, six rules) was flown with a clear of one free reading, and its whole argument for the memory was that a cell cleared by one miss is re-marked at once. **A hold removes that argument**: a held cell is not cleared by a miss, so there is nothing to re-mark. If it meant follow-up 16 itself, its designated `m23e30` is an arm here and this can be read as that round's family; say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question, in two halves that pull apart.** (1) *The family may collapse*: the memory's advantage (re-marking at once) is gone, so a run of two, a 2-of-3 and a 2-of-4 may be one rule. (2) *Strictness may become affordable*: a 3-of-3 or a 3-of-4 rule removes far more false returns than a run of two, and its price in follow-up 11 was a slower re-mark that the hold now makes unnecessary; but the price at the first marking is still paid (a cell the sonar sees 60% of the time is confirmed by 3-of-4 only 47% of the time at the first four readings), and a dropout that begins before the confirmation hides an obstacle from the hold altogether (follow-up 16's tension).

**The rule** (follow-up 16's detector with the confirmation varied). Each cell keeps a window of its last `N` readings (a reading counted at each ping that ensonifies it) and `occ`, the number of occupied readings since it was last cleared. A free cell is **marked** when at least `M` of its window were occupied (`occ` then starts at that count); a marked cell is **cleared** after `min(30, occ)` consecutive free readings (follow-up 12's evidence hold with a cap of 30, at every range). `(1, 1)` is follow-up 12's `e30`.

**Conditions** (follow-up 4's white faults, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 19):
* **`WD30`**: misses 0.2, first surfaces only, a white 2 degree heading error and the false rate **0.01** uniform, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law (follow-up 16's).
* **`VD30`**: the same with the misses at **0.4** (follow-up 11's `V`, with the dropouts): the sonar misses most of what it should see.
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the evidence hold of cap 30): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; the six rules **`m11e30`** (a mark of one: follow-up 12's `e30`), **`m22e30`** and **`m33e30`** (runs of two and three), **`m23e30`** (follow-up 16's designated), **`m24e30`** and **`m34e30`** (**the designated: 3 of the last 4**) under `WD30` and under `VD30` (12 arms); and `m34e30` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD25 = seeds 2501 to 2560 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m34e30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `WD30` and under `VD30`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (The designated is the bet that strictness is affordable; the bar is the one `m23e30` would be asked to meet, so a result of PARTLY or NOT with `m23e30` holding it is a finding about the bet, not the pair.)

**The tension, said before the data.** Three things pull on the choice. The hold makes the *memory* worthless and the *strictness* free of one of its two prices; the other price (the delay at first marking, which is longest where the misses are many) is untouched; and the dropouts are long enough that a delayed marking can mean a hidden obstacle. If the delay is the cost that matters, `VD30` is where the strict rules fall; if the phantoms are, `WD30` is where they win.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **Y1** the family collapses under a hold: under `WD30`, the five confirming rules (`m22e30`, `m33e30`, `m23e30`, `m24e30`, `m34e30`) have pooled counts within 20 of each other (largest minus smallest at most 20). (The memory's re-mark is moot; what is left is the phantom filter, and all five filter.)
* **Y2** every confirmation still repairs the white false returns: under `WD30`, each of the five reaches at least 20 more than `m11e30`. (Follow-up 7's G1 and follow-up 11's N4, with the hold.)
* **Y3** the memory is worth nothing once there is a hold: under `VD30`, `m23e30` is within 8 of `m22e30` (either side). (Follow-up 11's N1 predicted five more for the memory with a clear of one; a held cell is not cleared by a miss.)
* **Y4** strictness pays under mild misses: under `WD30`, `m34e30` reaches more than `m23e30`.
* **Y5** and costs under many: under `VD30`, `m34e30` reaches at least 5 fewer than `m23e30`. (A hit chance of 0.6 meets a 3-of-4 rule 47% of the time and a 2-of-3 rule 65%.)
* **Y6** `m34e30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `WD30`.
* **Y7** and under `VD30`.
* **Y8** on a sea with no faults `m34e30` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m34e30` against `m23e30` under `WD30` per kind, the per-gap counts and the number of obstacles marked more than ten pings late are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 3-of-4 is the best strict rule (a 4-of-5 or a likelihood sum is untried), or that a cap of 30 and a hold at every range are the right hold (follow-ups 13, 15, 17 and 18 are the rest of that table); that the readings of a cell are independent of one another; that the numbers carry to the persistent faults of follow-up 5 (which repeat by design and pass a confirmation). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 20 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD25 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `m11e30@WD30` 13/18; `m22e30@WD30` 14/18; `m33e30@WD30` 14/18; `m23e30@WD30` 12/18; `m24e30@WD30` 16/18; `m34e30@WD30` 14/18; `m11e30@VD30` 11/18; `m22e30@VD30` 16/18; `m33e30@VD30` 12/18; `m23e30@VD30` 14/18; `m24e30@VD30` 15/18; `m34e30@VD30` 16/18; `m34e30@clean` 17/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

*Reported and not predicted* in the registration was *the number of obstacles marked more than ten pings late*. It has no operational definition in the flight record (an obstacle's first possible sighting is not recorded) and **is not computed**; no prediction or verdict depends on it. The record does carry `detStats` (marks, clears, ghost marks, ghost refusals) and the counts, medians and gap widths the other studies print.

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `m34e30`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD25 = seeds 2501 to 2560 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 20 (2026-11-03; HELD25 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorConfirmHoldHeld25.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: NOT SUPPORTED (2 of 6 cells).** The designated `m34e30` against 80% of exact's count: WD30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current HELD; VD30: islands MISSED, gaps MISSED, current MISSED. **5 of 8 predictions held; Y4, Y6, Y7 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 176 (59/59/58); `long+fix+forget` 171 (58/57/56); `m11e30@WD30` 106 (34/36/36); `m22e30@WD30` 137 (49/37/51); `m33e30@WD30` 134 (51/33/50); `m23e30@WD30` 138 (51/38/49); `m24e30@WD30` 140 (51/37/52); `m34e30@WD30` 136 (53/32/51); `m11e30@VD30` 109 (37/36/36); `m22e30@VD30` 143 (53/42/48); `m33e30@VD30` 130 (46/38/46); `m23e30@VD30` 139 (52/40/47); `m24e30@VD30` 134 (54/35/45); `m34e30@VD30` 128 (46/37/45); `m34e30@clean` 163 (53/58/52).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **Y1 HELD**: the family collapses under a hold: under WD30, the five confirming rules have pooled counts within 20 of each other. m22e30 137, m33e30 134, m23e30 138, m24e30 140, m34e30 136: 6 apart (within 20 asked).
* **Y2 HELD**: every confirmation still repairs the white false returns: under WD30 each of the five reaches at least 20 more than m11e30. m22e30 137, m33e30 134, m23e30 138, m24e30 140, m34e30 136 against m11e30@WD30 106 (20 asked).
* **Y3 HELD**: the memory is worth nothing once there is a hold: under VD30, m23e30 is within 8 of m22e30. m23e30@VD30 139, m22e30@VD30 143: 4 apart, within 8 asked.
* **Y4 MISSED**: strictness pays under mild misses: under WD30, m34e30 reaches more than m23e30. m34e30@WD30 136 against m23e30@WD30 138.
* **Y5 HELD**: and costs under many: under VD30, m34e30 reaches at least 5 fewer than m23e30. m23e30@VD30 139, m34e30@VD30 128: +11 against the 5 asked.
* **Y6 MISSED**: m34e30 holds the bar in every kind under WD30. m34e30@WD30: 53/47.2 32/47.2 51/46.4 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **Y7 MISSED**: and under VD30. m34e30@VD30: 46/47.2 37/47.2 45/46.4 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **Y8 HELD**: on a sea with no faults m34e30 is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. m34e30@clean 163, long+fix+forget 171: 8 apart, within 8 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **With the evidence hold in place the confirmation family is one rule, and the bar was out of reach of every member.** The designated `m34e30` (3 of the last 4 to mark, the evidence hold of cap 30) holds **2 of the six bar cells**: **NOT SUPPORTED**. Under WD30 islands 53 and current 51 held, the gaps kind 32 against the 47.2 needed missed; under VD30 islands 46 (47.2 needed), gaps 37 and current 45 (46.4 needed) all missed, the first and last by about one flight. Five of eight predictions held (Y1, Y2, Y3, Y5, Y8). Y1 held by a wide margin: under WD30 the five confirming rules reach `m22e30` 137, `m33e30` 134, `m23e30` 138, `m24e30` 140, `m34e30` 136 of 180, **a spread of 6 where 20 was allowed** (exact 176, clean `long+fix+forget` 171). Y3 held: under VD30 `m23e30` 139 and `m22e30` 143, 4 apart where 8 was allowed, so the memory of follow-up 11 buys nothing once a held cell is not cleared by a miss.
* **A confirmation of any strength repairs the false returns, and a mark of one does not.** Y2 held with room: under WD30 each of the five reaches 28 to 34 more than `m11e30` (106; follow-up 12's `e30`); POST HOC `m34e30` against `m11e30` +48 / +18 (p 0.0003), while `m34e30` against each of the other four is level (+17 / +18 p 1.0 against `m22e30`, +16 / +14 p 0.86 against `m33e30`, +19 / +21 p 0.87 against `m23e30`, +16 / +20 p 0.62 against `m24e30`; 3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios, no correction for the number of tests).
* **Strictness costs where the misses are many and pays nothing where they are few.** Y4 missed: under WD30 `m34e30` 136 against `m23e30` 138 (-2). Y5 held: under VD30 `m34e30` 128 against `m23e30` 139 (-11), and `m33e30` is as low at 130 while `m22e30` (143) and `m23e30` (139) lead; POST HOC `m34e30` against `m22e30` under VD30 +14 / +29 (p 0.0315) and against `m23e30` +18 / +29 (p 0.14), the five rules' VD30 spread 15 against 6 under WD30. This is in the direction the registered tension named (the delay at first marking is longest where the misses are many); the delay itself was not measured, since the late-mark count was not computed (Amendment 1).
* **The gaps kind decides the bar, and it is not the confirmation that decides it.** POST HOC, no arm held the gaps cell under either condition: gaps reached out of 60 were 32 to 38 under WD30 and 35 to 42 under VD30 for all six rules (the best, 42, against the 47.2 needed), the mark of one included; islands and current sit at 45 to 54 for the five confirming rules. The narrowest gaps are where the strictest rules are worst: 24 m gaps reached under WD30 `m22e30` 5, `m33e30` 3, `m23e30` 6, `m24e30` 6, **`m34e30` 1**, under VD30 8, 4, 8, 6, **2** (of 20; exact 19, the 3-of-4 rule on a clean sea 18). POST HOC the rules that would have held 4 of the 6 cells, islands and current under both conditions, are `m22e30` and `m23e30`; `m24e30` 3, `m33e30` and `m34e30` 2, `m11e30` 0: among the five confirming rules the choice moves up to two of the four islands and current cells, and the two gaps cells are held by none. Not isolated by an arm: the same shortfall in the gaps kind appeared for `m23e30` under WD30 in follow-up 16's set (29 of 60 there, 38 here, different seeds).
* **A sea with no faults costs the strict rule a little.** Y8 held at the edge: `m34e30@clean` 163 against the clean map's 171, 8 apart where 8 was allowed (POST HOC +7 / +15, p 0.13; islands 53 against 58, current 52 against 56, gaps 58 against 57).

**What it does not say.** That 3-of-4 is the worst strict rule or 2-of-2 the best one (the five are level to within noise under WD30, and the pooled counts of six arms of one set of 180 scenarios do not rank them); that a hold of cap 30 at every range is right (follow-ups 13, 15, 17 and 18 vary it); that the gaps shortfall is the hold's, the dropouts' or the confirmation's (no arm here separates them). The bar was set at the designated's own cells, and it is the designated that fell short; `m23e30` held to the same bar would have been PARTLY SUPPORTED, post hoc. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 21: THE CLEAR THAT NEEDS CONFIRMING (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-confirm-hold"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 20 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. Follow-up 18 already took `hold-confirm-range-hold` as a second hold *on what was confirmed* (the ghost), so the trailing `-hold` is not taken that way again. It is taken here as the third item in the name read as a **hold given the confirmation's treatment**: a **hold, a confirmation and a hold that is itself confirmed**. The detector of follow-up 16 confirms its *mark* (`M` of the last `N` readings) and holds its *clear* as a run (`min(30, occ)` free readings in a row); follow-ups 12, 16 and 18 each name the same thing untried: **a clear that is an `K`-of-`L` rule on the free readings** (*"a K-of-N free rule and a leaky counter are untried"*). This round is that clear. If it meant the ghost again, or something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question, and why a K-of-N clear is not the same thing as a shorter hold.** A run of `L` free readings is cleared only by `L` free in a row: one occupied reading anywhere in the stretch resets it. That is what makes the evidence hold long, and what makes **a cell that flickers occupied** immortal. The one flickering cell the generator makes every time is the **fix smear**: the white 3 m fix shifts each ping's placement of a wall by a fraction of a 5 m cell, so a cell on the edge of a wall is read occupied on some pings and free on others, 20 to 40% of the time. Under a run it never gets thirty free in a row, and a wall held for thirty readings is a wall thickened by the smear (follow-up 13's cost of a hold at range, follow-up 2's of a sticky map). A `K`-of-`L` clear with `K` = 0.8 `L` clears a cell that reads free four times in five (an edge cell) and keeps one that reads occupied four times in five (a wall's core). **It is the smear cure for a long hold, if the smear is what it costs.** The price is the other flickering cell: **a dropout that leaks**.

**The rule** (follow-up 16's designated detector, 2-of-3 to mark and the evidence hold, with the clear changed). Each cell keeps `occ` (occupied readings since last cleared) and a window of its last 32 readings (a reading counted at each ping that ensonifies it). A marked cell is cleared when, among its last `L = min(30, occ)` readings, at least `K = ceil(f x L)` were free; `f = 1` is the run of follow-ups 12 and 16 (`L` of the last `L` free), `f` less than 1 tolerates occupied readings in the stretch. For a phantom (`occ` 1) `L` is 1 and the rule is one free reading whatever `f`.

**Conditions** (follow-up 4's white faults, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 20):
* **`WD30`**: misses 0.2, first surfaces only, a white 2 degree heading error and the false rate 0.01 uniform, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law (follow-up 16's).
* **`WD30L`**: the same with the dropouts **leaking**: while an obstacle is dropped out each reading of it is still taken as a normal return with chance **0.15**, a flickering shadow (a new module option, off in every other condition).
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix, a 2-of-3 mark and the hold of cap 30 at every range): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `WD30` and under `WD30L` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23`** (2-of-3 to mark, one free reading to clear), **`f100`** (the run: follow-up 16's `m23e30`), **`f90`**, **`f80`** (**the designated**) and **`f60`** (12 arms); and `f80` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD26 = seeds 2601 to 2660 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `f80` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `WD30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (As in follow-up 16 the bar is not relative to the run: a result of PARTLY or NOT with `f100` holding it is a finding about the clear; predictions A1 to A4 are the comparison.)

**The tension, said before the data.** Two things pull on `f`. The smear cure needs a clear that tolerates an occupied reading in five, and a tolerance that large is a **shorter hold against exactly the fault the hold is for**: a dropout is a stretch of all-free readings, and `f` = 0.8 clears at 24 free of 30 against the run's 30, six readings (18 m of travel) sooner. And a real far cell the sonar sees only 60% of the time (the long sonar's edge, misses growing with range) is free 40% of the time: `f` = 0.6 clears it, which is the thinning of the real wall the other end of the range. The pair of conditions is built to find both prices.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **A1** the K-of-N clear cures the smear where it hurts: in `hard-gaps` under `WD30`, `f80` reaches at least 3 more than `f100`. (The 24 m gaps are the ones a thickened wall closes.)
* **A2** and pooled: under `WD30`, `f80` reaches at least 5 more than `f100`.
* **A3** the cure has a limit: under `WD30`, `f60` reaches at least 5 fewer than `f80`. (It clears real cells read occupied 40 to 60% of the time at the sonar's edge.)
* **A4** the leak is the price: under `WD30L`, `f80` reaches at least 5 fewer than `f100`. (A leaking dropout reads free 85% of the time: the run never gets its 30, the K-of-N clear gets its 24 of 30.)
* **A5** the run is not troubled by the leak: `f100` under `WD30L` is within 10 of `f100` under `WD30` (either side).
* **A6** `f80` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `WD30`.
* **A7** and under `WD30L`.
* **A8** on a sea with no faults `f80` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`f90` and `m23` against the rest, the per-kind and per-gap counts, and the pooled number of occupied cells at the last plan (the smear, measured) are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 0.8, 0.6 and a leak of 0.15 are the right numbers (the leak is a made-up flicker, not a measured one); that the window of 32 and the cap of 30 are the right sizes; that the K-of-N clear is the best cure for the smear (a filtered fix, a thinning of the map, a range gate on the hold — follow-up 15 — and the leaky counter are untried); that the fix smear is all there is to a flickering cell. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

### AMENDMENT 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 21 (2026-11-03; written AFTER the general detector (`brain/subSensedDetect.mjs`), ping's dropout object and leak, and the study engine (`tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy.mjs`, specs `tools/ship/subSensedDetSpecs.mjs`) were gated and sabotaged and the DEV set was flown, and BEFORE HELD26 is flown; DEV is a sanity run, NOT scored, and nothing below moves a prediction)

**What was run.** The modules and the engine as logged in their gates' headers (the detector module 161 mutants, 152 red and 9 equivalent; ping's new lines 63 mutants, all red; the study engine and the 516 mechanical mutants of the specs, each logged in `tools/ship/subSensedDetStudy-selfcheck.mjs`); the specs checked line for line against the registrations; the DEV set (seeds 1 to 6 of each kind, 18 scenarios an arm; `--set dev` writes nothing). Neither module nor the engine was edited after its sabotage.

**What DEV showed, read as sanity only (reached of 18, the three kinds pooled):** `exact` 18/18; `long+fix+forget` 18/18; `inc@WD30` 8/18; `m23@WD30` 14/18; `f100@WD30` 12/18; `f90@WD30` 12/18; `f80@WD30` 13/18; `f60@WD30` 12/18; `inc@WD30L` 17/18; `m23@WD30L` 18/18; `f100@WD30L` 17/18; `f90@WD30L` 16/18; `f80@WD30L` 17/18; `f60@WD30L` 15/18; `f80@clean` 18/18. **The printed DEV verdict lines are not read**: the thresholds are made for 180 flights and mean nothing on 18. What DEV was read FOR is the plumbing: every arm of the spec was flown, none crashed or timed out wholesale, no arm that should differ from another is identical to it by construction (the detectors differ only in their rules, the faults in their presets).

**Unchanged.** The arms, the designated detector `f80`, the bar, the verdict rule, the predictions with their reasons, the set (HELD26 = seeds 2601 to 2660 of each of the three kinds) and its single reading are as registered above. Any post hoc test is labelled post hoc.

### RESULT OF FOLLOW-UP 21 (2026-11-03; HELD26 flown ONCE, 2,700 flights, `brain/subSensedDetDetectorHoldConfirmHoldHeld26.jsonl`; the verdict below is the one the study command printed; every number under "POST HOC" was computed after it was read and is labelled)

**VERDICT: PARTLY SUPPORTED (5 of 6 cells).** The designated `f80` against 80% of exact's count: WD30: islands HELD, gaps MISSED, current HELD; WD30L: islands HELD, gaps HELD, current HELD. **4 of 8 predictions held; A1, A2, A5, A6 missed.**

**Flown (reached of 180; islands / gaps / current):** `exact` 174 (60/58/56); `long+fix+forget` 172 (55/59/58); `inc@WD30` 86 (31/24/31); `m23@WD30` 138 (53/36/49); `f100@WD30` 147 (52/42/53); `f90@WD30` 145 (52/41/52); `f80@WD30` 142 (53/36/53); `f60@WD30` 134 (49/36/49); `inc@WD30L` 127 (35/57/35); `m23@WD30L` 162 (58/54/50); `f100@WD30L` 168 (57/58/53); `f90@WD30L` 168 (57/58/53); `f80@WD30L` 163 (53/55/55); `f60@WD30L` 159 (53/53/53); `f80@clean` 173 (57/60/56).

**The predictions (each scored as registered):**
* **A1 MISSED**: the K-of-N clear cures the smear where it hurts: in hard-gaps under WD30, f80 reaches at least 3 more than f100. f80@WD30[gaps] 36, f100@WD30[gaps] 42: -6 against the 3 asked.
* **A2 MISSED**: and pooled: under WD30, f80 reaches at least 5 more than f100. f80@WD30 142, f100@WD30 147: -5 against the 5 asked.
* **A3 HELD**: the cure has a limit: under WD30, f60 reaches at least 5 fewer than f80. f80@WD30 142, f60@WD30 134: +8 against the 5 asked.
* **A4 HELD**: the leak is the price: under WD30L, f80 reaches at least 5 fewer than f100. f100@WD30L 168, f80@WD30L 163: +5 against the 5 asked.
* **A5 MISSED**: the run is not troubled by the leak: f100 under WD30L is within 10 of f100 under WD30. f100@WD30L 168, f100@WD30 147: 21 apart, within 10 asked.
* **A6 MISSED**: f80 holds the bar in every kind under WD30. f80@WD30: 53/48.0 36/46.4 53/44.8 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **A7 HELD**: and under WD30L. f80@WD30L: 53/48.0 55/46.4 55/44.8 (reached / needed of 0.8 exact, islands gaps current).
* **A8 HELD**: on a sea with no faults f80 is within 8 of the clean long+fix+forget. f80@clean 173, long+fix+forget 172: 1 apart, within 8 asked.

**What was found (the registered measures; the reading of them is post hoc where said).**
* **The confirmed clear holds five of six bar cells, and nothing in the set says it is better than the run clear it replaces.** The designated `f80` (a 2-of-3 mark, the evidence hold of cap 30, a marked cell cleared when 0.8 of its last `min(30, occ)` readings were free) holds islands 53 and current 53 under WD30 and all three kinds under WD30L (53, 55, 55 against 48.0, 46.4 and 44.8 needed); the gaps kind under WD30 is 36 against the 46.4 needed: **PARTLY SUPPORTED**; four of eight predictions held (A3, A4, A7, A8). Counts of 180 under WD30: `f100` (the run clear, which is follow-up 16's `m23e30`) **147**, `f90` 145, `f80` 142, `f60` 134, `m23` (the mark alone) 138, `inc` 86 (exact 174); under WD30L: `f100` 168, `f90` 168, `f80` 163, `f60` 159, `m23` 162, `inc` 127. POST HOC (3 kinds pooled, 180 paired scenarios, no correction for the number of tests) `f80` against `f100` +7 / +12 under WD30 (p 0.36) and +4 / +9 under WD30L (p 0.27); against `m23` +25 / +21 (p 0.66) and +14 / +13 (p 1.0); against `inc` +63 / +7 and +46 / +10 (both p below 0.0001).
* **The smear cure is not seen (A1 and A2 missed).** The registered claim was that a clear that tolerates one occupied reading in five thins the walls the hold thickens, and pays in the gaps kind under WD30. Gaps reached under WD30 (of 60): `f100` 42, `f90` 41, `f80` 36, `f60` 36, `m23` 36 (exact 58): `f80` is **6 below** `f100` where 3 above was asked, and pooled it is 5 below where 5 above was asked. The 24 m gaps reached (of 20; exact 18): `f100` 9, `f90` 8, `f80` 7, `f60` 6, `m23` 6, `inc` 6. A different reading of the medians is consistent with the mechanism and not with a payoff: the median number of occupied cells at the last plan under WD30 is `m23` 36, `f100` 74, `f90` 74, `f80` 71, `f60` 61 (the hold thickens the map and the tolerant clears thin it a little), and reach does not follow it. No arm isolates the smear, so this is not a test of it.
* **The limit and the price are in the registered direction, and thin.** A3 held: `f60` 134 against `f80` 142 under WD30 (8 fewer, 5 asked; POST HOC `f80` against `f60` +27 / +19, p 0.30). A4 held at its edge: under WD30L `f80` 163 against `f100` 168, **exactly the 5 fewer asked** (`f60` 159; the order `f100` = `f90` > `f80` > `f60` is monotone in the tolerance; POST HOC +4 / +9, p 0.27).
* **A5 missed because the leak helps every arm.** The registered reason (a run is not troubled by the leak) is not what the data show: `f100` is **168 under WD30L against 147 under WD30**, 21 apart where 10 were allowed, and the map that forgets goes from 86 to 127. The leaking dropouts return some readings, so the same dropouts are easier on the vessel than leak-free ones in every arm. Under WD30L the gaps kind is held by every arm, `inc` included (57 of 60).
* **A sea with no faults costs nothing.** A8 held: `f80@clean` 173 against the clean map's 172 (POST HOC +6 / +5, p 1.0; all three kinds hold the bar).

**What it does not say.** That a K-of-L clear cannot cure the smear: the set shows no gain at 0.8, 0.9 or 0.6 in a regime where the gaps kind is limited by the faults and the hold together, and the cell the cure was registered for (gaps under WD30) is the one where no confirming arm reaches the bar. That 0.8, 30 and a leak of 0.15 are the right numbers (the leak is made up, and the data show it is the easier sea). The thickness of the walls was not measured directly. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 22: WHICH CONFIRMATION WHEN THE CLEAR IS CONFIRMED TOO (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-confirm-hold-confirm"; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, and registered while follow-ups 7 to 21 were unflown, so nothing below was shaped by their results)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 21's name (`hold-confirm-hold`) with the first word moved to the front, the move follow-up 20 made on follow-up 16 and follow-up 15 on follow-up 13: **the confirmation of the mark is the subject, the hold and its confirmed clear are in place**. Follow-up 20 asked which mark rule to use when the hold is there, with a clear that is a run of free readings (which a flickering cell never meets); follow-up 21 gave the clear a confirmation (`K` of the last `min(30, occ)` readings free). **This round asks follow-up 20's question of follow-up 21's detector.** The answer can differ, and for a reason that did not exist in follow-up 20: **with a clear that tolerates an occupied reading, a flickering cell is cleared and must be marked again, so the mark rule decides how fast the smear's edge cells come back** (follow-up 21's mechanism, seen from the mark). If it meant the symmetric rule of a radar's track initiation and deletion, the same `M`-of-`N` at both ends on short fixed windows (a related reading, left out), or something else, say so before the build, which waits for follow-up 7's set to be flown.

**The question in numbers.** A cell the sonar sees steadily (a wall's core, occupied 80% of the readings) and a cell on the edge of the fix smear (occupied 30%) pass the rules at these rates per reading: a run of two 64% and 9%, a run of three 51% and 2.7%, a 2-of-3 90% and 22%, a 2-of-4 about 97% and 35%, a 3-of-4 82% and 8.4%. **The rate at which each rule separates the wall from the smear** (steady over flicker) is 7, 19, 4, 3 and 10 times: the run of three separates best and is the slowest to mark a real cell. With a clear that cleans the smear (follow-up 21) a rule that does not re-mark the edge cells keeps it clean; with a run-clear (follow-up 20) the edge cells that are marked stay marked, and what mattered was only whether they were marked at all.

**The rule** (follow-up 21's detector with the mark varied). Each cell keeps a window of its last `N` readings (a reading counted at each ping that ensonifies it; the clear's own window is the last 32), `occ` (occupied readings since last cleared). A free cell is **marked** when at least `M` of its last `N` readings were occupied (`occ` then starts at that count); a marked cell is **cleared** when, among its last `L = min(30, occ)` readings, at least `K = ceil(0.8 x L)` were free (follow-up 21's `f80`, at every range). `(1, 1)` is `f80` with a mark of one.

**Conditions** (exactly follow-up 21's, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 21): **`WD30`** (follow-up 4's white faults with the false rate 0.01 uniform, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law) and **`WD30L`** (the same with the dropouts leaking, each reading of a dropped-out obstacle still a normal return with chance 0.15), and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the clear of `f80`): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; the six rules **`m11f80`**, **`m22f80`** and **`m33f80`** (a mark of one, runs of two and three; **`m33f80` is the designated**), **`m23f80`** (follow-up 21's designated), **`m24f80`** and **`m34f80`** under `WD30` and under `WD30L` (12 arms); and `m33f80` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD27 = seeds 2701 to 2760 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m33f80` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `WD30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**Why `m33f80` is the designated, and the tension, said before the data.** It is the rule that separates the wall from the smear best, and follow-up 20's designated bet (`m34e30`, strictness affordable) is a different bet on a different clear. Against it: it is the slowest to mark a real cell (51% a reading at a hit chance of 0.8, 22% at 0.6), a dropout that begins before an obstacle is confirmed hides it from the hold altogether, and a leaking dropout clears cells that a run of three then takes three clean readings to bring back.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **X1** separating wall from smear beats the memory where the clear cleans: under `WD30`, `m33f80` reaches at least 5 more than `m23f80`. (A run of three re-marks an edge cell 2.7% of readings, a 2-of-3 rule 22%.)
* **X2** and the run's price shows where cells are cleared: under `WD30L`, `m33f80` reaches at least 3 fewer than `m23f80`. (The leaking dropout clears cells; a run of three is slow to bring them back.)
* **X3** unlike follow-up 20's, the family does not collapse: under `WD30`, the five confirming rules (`m22f80`, `m33f80`, `m23f80`, `m24f80`, `m34f80`) have pooled counts more than 10 apart (largest minus smallest). (A clear that cleans makes the mark rule the thing that keeps the smear out.)
* **X4** every confirmation still repairs the white false returns: under `WD30`, each of the five reaches at least 20 more than `m11f80`.
* **X5** the memory is worth something again: under `WD30L`, `m23f80` reaches at least 5 more than `m22f80`. (Follow-up 20's Y3 predicted nothing for the memory under a run-clear; here cells are cleared and have to be re-marked.)
* **X6** `m33f80` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `WD30`.
* **X7** and under `WD30L`.
* **X8** on a sea with no faults `m33f80` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m34f80` against `m33f80`, the per-kind and per-gap counts and the pooled number of occupied cells at the last plan are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 0.8, 30 and a leak of 0.15 are the right numbers (follow-up 21's caveats stand); that a run of three is the best of the family (a 4-of-5 or a likelihood sum is untried); that the separating ratio, which assumes the edge cell's 30% and the wall's 80% as independent readings, is what the flight sees (the vessel moves between pings). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.
